  "abstract_verbatim": "For each fixed positive disorder strength, we prove that the nearest-neighbor Anderson operator on the square lattice with independent uniform site potentials almost surely has pure-point spectral type throughout its spectrum. <a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For each fixed positive disorder strength, we prove that the nearest-neighbor Anderson operator on the square lattice with independent uniform site potentials almost surely has pure-point spectral type throughout its spectrum. This resolves the pure-point assertion of the two-dimensional Anderson localization conjecture for the uniform single-site law.

- **claim-planar-pure-point-spectrum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-main-theorem)): For each fixed $h>0$, almost surely $H_v$ has pure-point spectral type, admits a complete orthonormal eigenbasis, and has spectrum $$\sigma(H_v)=[-4-h,4+h].$$ Its eigenvalues are dense in this interval. The probability-one event in this statement is allowed to depend on $h$.
  - Explanation: For fixed positive disorder with independent uniform site potentials, the planar operator has a complete eigenbasis and dense eigenvalues throughout its deterministic spectrum. The almost-sure event may depend on the disorder strength.
- **claim-absorption-and-passive-schur-complements** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mat-ward)): Suppose $\mathscr L$ is invertible and $W=-\operatorname{Im}\mathscr L\ge YY^\top$. Then $$\begin{aligned} \operatorname{Im}G&=G^*WG=GWG^*, \label{mat:ward-identity}\\ \left\lVert Y^\top GYa\right\rVert^2 &\le \operatorname{Im}(a^*Y^\top GYa), \qquad \left\lVert Y^\top GY\right\rVert\le1. \label{mat:ward-contraction} \end{aligned}$$ For the physical system in [mat:response](#mat:response), $S$ is symmetric and unitary. If $\mathscr L$ is complex symmetric and passive, each defined Schur complement is complex symmetric and passive. An absorption lower bound supported on retained coordinates persists in the Schur complement.
  - Explanation: Absorption controls projected resolvent responses. In the physical system it gives symmetric unitary scattering, while Schur elimination preserves symmetry, passivity and retained absorption.
- **claim-common-phase-port-closure** (construction, [source](#stmt-mat-closure)): Partition the ports into retained ports and a set $C$ to be closed, and write $$S=\begin{pmatrix}A&T\\T^\top&B\end{pmatrix}.$$ Close every port in $C$ and add the real shift $w\sigma_j$ to the physical diagonal for each closed port $j$ of strength $\sigma_j$. Where the resulting inverse exists, its scattering matrix on the retained ports is $$S_z=A+Tz(\mathbf 1-zB)^{-1}T^\top, \qquad z=\frac{w+i}{w-i}. \label{mat:closure-formula}$$ The right-hand side is analytic for $\left\lvert z\right\rvert<1$.
  - Explanation: Closing a prescribed port block with a common normalized real shift gives an explicit rational scattering family. Contractivity of the closed block ensures analyticity inside the disk.
- **claim-conditional-single-port-energy-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-mat-energy-closure)): Consider closing a single port $a$. Set $$u=S_{\widehat a,a},\qquad b=S_{aa},\qquad \gamma=\frac{z}{1-zb},$$ where $\widehat a$ means that port $a$ is omitted. Let $\mathcal F$ contain the open system and the real data $f$, before the closing value is sampled. Assume that its conditional law assigns no mass to the finite exceptional set in [mat:closure](#mat:closure). If $\left\lvert b\right\rvert<1$, put $\mu=\mathbb E[\gamma\mid\mathcal F]$. Then $$\mathbb E[\mathcal Q_{S_z}(f_{\widehat a})\mid\mathcal F] \le \mathcal Q_S(f)+2\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert\,\mathcal M_a(f). \label{mat:conditional-comparison}$$ If $\left\lvert b\right\rvert=1$, the same assertion holds with zero error, and we use the convention $\mu=0$.
  - Explanation: The conditional mean of the closing coefficient bounds the expected energy increase. A decoupled port has zero closing error.
- **claim-cutoff-cauchy-error-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-mat-cutoff)): Fix a smooth function $\chi:\mathbb R\to[0,1]$ equal to one near zero and supported in a fixed sufficiently small interval about zero. For a port of strength $\sigma$, give its hidden real shift $\delta$ the law $$\frac1{Z_\sigma}\chi(\delta) \frac{\sigma\,\,\mathrm d\delta}{\pi(\delta^2+\sigma^2)}, \qquad Z_\sigma=\int_\mathbb R\chi(\delta) \frac{\sigma\,\,\mathrm d\delta}{\pi(\delta^2+\sigma^2)}. \label{mat:cutoff-law}$$ Use $w=\delta/\sigma$ in [mat:closure](#mat:closure). Conditional on the open system, let $g=\sigma G_{xx}$, where $x$ is the site of the port. For all sufficiently small $\sigma$, the mean in [mat:energy-closure](#mat:energy-closure) satisfies $$\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert\le C,\qquad \left\lvert\mu\right\rvert\le C\sqrt\sigma\quad\hbox{if }\left\lvert g\right\rvert>\sqrt\sigma. \label{mat:cutoff-estimate}$$ The constants depend on $\chi$, not on the exterior system.
  - Explanation: A compactly supported cutoff of the Cauchy closing law has a uniformly bounded mean error, with improved smallness when the normalized local response exceeds the specified threshold.
- **claim-dimension-independent-weak-resolvent-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-mat-weak)): Let $B$ be a contraction and let $U,V$ be compatible deterministic matrices. At the almost-everywhere boundary values of the rational function in question, $$\left\\|\left\lVert U(\mathbf 1-zB)^{-1}V\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\right\\|_{L^{1,\infty}} \le C\left\lVert U\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\left\lVert V\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}. \label{mat:weak-sandwich}$$ For any compatible square matrix $D$, $$\left\\|\left\lvert\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\bigl(D(\mathbf 1-zB)^{-1}\bigr)\right\rvert\right\\|_{L^{1,\infty}} \le C\left\lVert D\right\rVert_1. \label{mat:weak-trace}$$ Here and below $\left\lVert D\right\rVert_1$ denotes the trace norm. In particular, every moment of order $0<p<1$ is bounded by $C_p$ times the corresponding right-hand scale to the power $p$.
  - Explanation: Sandwiched resolvents and inverse traces obey weak integral bounds controlled by Hilbert–Schmidt and trace norms rather than matrix dimension. Integrating their tails gives fractional moments.
- **claim-determinant-phase-integration** (bound, [source](#stmt-mat-phase)): Let $D(s)$ be a continuously differentiable $d\times d$ matrix on a bounded real interval, with $-\operatorname{Im}D(s)>0$. Its determinant has a continuous phase $\Phi$ such that $$-d\pi<\Phi(s)<0,\qquad \Phi'(s)=\operatorname{Im}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\bigl(D'(s)D(s)^{-1}\bigr). \label{mat:phase-derivative}$$ Suppose, in particular, that $$D'(s)=P_J-i\tau\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}_J(a_i'(s)),\qquad 0\le\tau\le1,\qquad \left\lvert a_i'(s)\right\rvert^2\le C a_i(s),$$ where $P_J$ is the coordinate projection onto $J$, and that $-\operatorname{Im}D(s)\ge\tau\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}_J(a_i(s))$. With $G=D^{-1}$, $$\Phi'(s)\ge\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_J\operatorname{Im}G(s)-C'd. \label{mat:phase-lower}$$ Consequently, on an interval $[a,b]$, $$\int_a^b\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_J\operatorname{Im}D(s)^{-1}\,\,\mathrm ds \le C''d(1+b-a). \label{mat:phase-integral}$$ The integral bound remains valid for passive matrices at their almost-everywhere existing inverses, by strict regularization.
  - Explanation: A continuous determinant phase controls integrated inverse traces along a real shift, including profile-dependent artificial absorption. Strict regularization extends the integral estimate to passive matrices.
- **claim-clipped-boundary-coupling** (construction, [source](#stmt-cell-geometry)): The set $Z$ consists of the four sides of the square with their corners removed. Every site of $Z$ has an inward nearest neighbor in $J\setminus Z$. The restriction $M:P\longrightarrow\mathbb R^Z$ is an isomorphism whose singular values lie in $[1,\sqrt3]$. In particular, $$\left\lVert M\right\rVert\le\sqrt3,\qquad \left\lVert Mw\right\rVert\ge\left\lVert w\right\rVert\quad(w\in P).$$ There is an orthogonal decomposition $P=P_0+Q$: $P_0$ is the coordinate projection onto the single side sites of $B$, and $Q$ is the direct sum of four two-dimensional corner planes.
  - Explanation: Clipping the corners gives an inward neighbor at every coupled boundary site. The boundary coupling is invertible on its row space, with a separate plane for each corner.
- **claim-profile-parameter-with-controlled-inverse** (construction, [source](#stmt-cell-profile)): There is a fixed exponent $C_2$ such that, for every sufficiently large $L$, a deterministic $u_L\in[-c_u/L,c_u/L]$ can be chosen for which the real symmetric Dirichlet matrix $$A^0=\operatorname{Adj}_J+\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}_J(v^0-E)$$ satisfies $\left\lVert(A^0)^{-1}\right\rVert\le L^{C_2}$. The bounds in [cell:profile-size](#cell:profile-size), [cell:design-margin](#cell:design-margin) hold for this choice, with constants independent of $L$.
  - Explanation: A deterministic profile deformation can avoid the small eigenvalues of the punctured Dirichlet matrix while preserving the designed decay and potential margins.
- **claim-branch-law-stability-and-likelihood** (construction, [source](#stmt-cell-branch)): For sufficiently large $L$, the above choices have the following properties, uniformly over permitted corner boxes:* 1. *Every interior draw has $$\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert\le2L^{C_2},\qquad \left\lVert-A^{-1}p-\psi\|_J\right\rVert\le C L^{C_2+1}\epsilon. \label{cell:residual}$$ The exponents in these bounds are independent of $D_2$, provided $D_3$ is subsequently chosen sufficiently large.* 2. *All true potentials on $V$ lie in a fixed compact subinterval of $(-h,h)$. The root center satisfies $$\|c(v)-v_0^0\|\le C L^{C_2+1}\epsilon. \label{cell:root-calibration}$$ The plateau of $\vartheta$ can therefore be fixed to contain every true value on $V$ and every single side value in $B$, for all the cells used. The artificial absorptions on these sites are constant as $r$, the jitters, the common side shift, and the hidden root draw vary.* 3. *Relative to the true product law on the tile conditioned on these same four corner boxes, the branch has a density $\mathcal L$ satisfying $$0\le\mathcal L\le \exp\bigl(C L^3+C L^2\log L\bigr), \qquad \int\mathcal L\,\,\mathrm d\mathbb P_{\rm true,boxes}=1. \label{cell:likelihood}$$ The corner-box conditional laws and the laws outside $V\cup B$ are unchanged.* *Artificial probes may have any prescribed mask of absent, constant, or profile-dependent strengths, as in [mat:probes](#mat:probes). For subsequent cell estimates their common scale is restricted to $0\le\tau\le e^{-c_*L}$, with a fixed $c_*>2\nu$.
  - Explanation: The branch sampler preserves a controlled interior inverse, keeps true cell potentials in a compact interior interval and has a bounded likelihood relative to the conditioned true law. Its internal probe strengths remain constant during the relevant sampling.
- **claim-response-weighted-projected-impedance** (bound, [source](#stmt-imp-weighted)): Let $T$ be any fixed complex symmetric matrix on the boundary space with $-\operatorname{Im}T\ge0$, and let $k$ be any fixed vector there. For each boundary site prescribe $a_i$ to be one of $0$, $1$, and $\vartheta$, with the prescription independent of the boundary variables. Set $$\mathcal T=T+\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}_B\bigl(v_i-i\tau a_i(v_i)\bigr), \qquad \rho=\left\lVert P(\mathcal T-iP)^{-1}k\right\rVert^2. \label{imp:rho}$$ Fix $c_*>0$ and suppose $0\le\tau\le e^{-c_*L}$. There are constants $c>0$, $C<\infty$, and a sufficiently large lower threshold for $L$ such that $$\mathbb E_B\!\left[ \rho\,\mathbf 1_{\{\left\lVert P\mathcal T^{-1}P\right\rVert\le C(1+L)^C\}} \right] \ge c\,\mathbb E_B\rho. \label{imp:weighted-estimate}$$ Here the expectation uses the boundary law conditional on its four corner boxes. The constants are uniform in all the exterior data just described. Moreover, changing only the single side variables to any other bounded allowed settings, including their artificial absorptions, changes $\rho$ by at most fixed multiplicative factors, pointwise wherever the inverses exist.
  - Explanation: A polynomially controlled projected boundary inverse retains a fixed fraction of response weight even for arbitrary passive exterior data. Bounded changes of the single-side settings preserve the weight up to fixed factors.
- **claim-comparison-of-absorbed-responses** (bound, [source](#stmt-imp-comparison)): Suppose $D_1,D_2$ are invertible matrices with $-\operatorname{Im}D_j\ge P$. If $D_2-D_1=P\Delta P$ and $\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert\le K$, then, for every $k$, $$(1+K)^{-1}\left\lVert PD_1^{-1}k\right\rVert \le \left\lVert PD_2^{-1}k\right\rVert \le (1+K)\left\lVert PD_1^{-1}k\right\rVert.$$
  - Explanation: A bounded perturbation supported on coordinates carrying unit absorption changes the projected response by only a bounded multiplicative factor.
- **claim-polynomial-norm-and-product-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-imp-polynomials)): On a rectangular box in a fixed number of real variables, let $p$ be a polynomial of degree at most $d$ with values in a finite-dimensional Hilbert space. There is a constant depending only on $d$ and the number of variables such that $$\sup\left\lVert p\right\rVert^2\le C\,\mathop{\mathrm{avg}}\left\lVert p\right\rVert^2. \label{imp:poly-average}$$ If the length of a coordinate interval is at least $\ell$, then $$\sup\left\lVert\partial_jp\right\rVert\le C\ell^{-1}\sup\left\lVert p\right\rVert. \label{imp:poly-derivative}$$ If $q$ is a scalar polynomial of degree at most $d$, then $$\sup\left\lVert pq\right\rVert\ge c\,\sup\left\lVert p\right\rVert\,\sup\left\lvert q\right\rvert. \label{imp:poly-product}$$ The constants do not depend on the dimension of the target Hilbert space or the side lengths of the box.
  - Explanation: Bounded-degree polynomial norm comparisons, derivative estimates and product lower bounds remain independent of the target Hilbert-space dimension and box side lengths.
- **claim-resolution-of-weighted-slices** (bound, [source](#stmt-imp-resolution)): Let $I$ be an interval of length at least $\ell$, let $N$ be a Hilbert-space-valued polynomial of fixed degree on $I$, and let $z$ be a continuously differentiable complex function satisfying $$\left\lvert z(s)\right\rvert\ge c_0 a, \qquad \left\lvert z'(s)\right\rvert\le C_0, \qquad a>0.$$ Then $$\sup_{s\in I}\frac{\left\lVert N(s)\right\rVert^2}{\left\lvert z(s)\right\rvert^2} \le \frac{C}{\min(\ell,a)} \int_I\frac{\left\lVert N(s)\right\rVert^2}{\left\lvert z(s)\right\rvert^2}\,\,\mathrm ds.$$ Here $C$ depends only on $c_0,C_0$ and the degree bound.
  - Explanation: A lower denominator scale and an upper derivative bound limit the concentration of a polynomial numerator divided by that denominator. This includes maxima at interval endpoints.
- **claim-corner-schur-derivatives-and-absorption-floor** (bound, [source](#stmt-imp-schur-estimates)): Uniformly throughout the box, $$\begin{aligned} \operatorname{Im}e&\ge\left\lVert d_1\right\rVert^2, &\left\lvert\partial_s e\right\rvert&\le C\tau(1+\left\lVert d_1\right\rVert), \label{imp:derivatives-a}\\ \left\lVert\partial_s d_1\right\rVert&\le C\tau, &\left\lvert\partial_s\beta\right\rvert&\le C\tau\left\lVert a\right\rVert, \label{imp:derivatives-b}\\ \left\lVert\partial_{t_j}d_1\right\rVert&\le C, &\left\lvert\partial_{t_j}e\right\rvert&\le C(1+\left\lVert d_1\right\rVert). \label{imp:derivatives-c} \end{aligned}$$ If at least one artificial probe is present in the tested triple and $\tau>0$, then also $$\operatorname{Im}e\ge\frac{\tau}{\operatorname{poly}(L)}. \label{imp:probe-floor}$$ For every fixed $t$, $$\int\frac{\left\lVert d_1(s,t)\right\rVert^2}{\left\lvert s-e(s,t)\right\rvert^2}\,\,\mathrm ds \le\int\operatorname{Im}\frac1{s-e(s,t)}\,\,\mathrm ds \le\operatorname{poly}(L). \label{imp:s-integral}$$
  - Explanation: Absorption links the Schur denominator width to the projected coupling. Supported derivatives give uniform variation bounds, and an active artificial probe supplies an additional absorption floor.
- **claim-elimination-of-missing-boundary-directions** (construction, [source](#stmt-imp-schur-interface)): Let $N=\mathbf 1-P$ on the boundary space. Almost everywhere in the boundary law, the $N,N$ block of $\mathcal T$ is invertible. Define its Schur matrix and transformed source by $$\begin{aligned} \widehat T &=P\mathcal TP-P\mathcal TN(N\mathcal TN)^{-1}N\mathcal TP,\\ \widehat k &=Pk-P\mathcal TN(N\mathcal TN)^{-1}Nk. \end{aligned}$$ Then $\widehat T$ is complex symmetric and passive, and $$\begin{aligned} \widehat T^{-1}&=P\mathcal T^{-1}P,\\ \widehat k&=\widehat T P\mathcal T^{-1}k,\\ P(\mathcal T-iP)^{-1}k&=(\widehat T-i\mathbf 1_P)^{-1}\widehat k. \end{aligned}$$ In particular $\rho=\left\lVert(\widehat T-i\mathbf 1_P)^{-1}\widehat k\right\rVert^2$, and <a href="#imp:weighted-estimate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="imp:weighted-estimate">[imp:weighted-estimate]</a> controls $\widehat T^{-1}$ on a set carrying a fixed fraction of this weight.
  - Explanation: Eliminating the missing directions produces a passive symmetric effective boundary matrix and transformed source. Its inverse and response weight match the projected quantities used by the impedance estimate.
- **claim-branch-transmission-and-relative-closing-error** (bound, [source](#stmt-trans-cell)): Choose the branch parameters in the order described below and fix $c_*>2\nu$. For $0\le\tau\le e^{-c_*L}$, uniformly in the fixed exterior data and allowed corner boxes, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb E\left\lvert t\right\rvert^2 &\ge e^{-C(\log L)^2}\,\mathbb E_B\rho, \label{trans:lower}\\ \mathbb E\bigl[\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert\,\left\lvert t\right\rvert^2\bigr] &\le e^{-cL}\,\mathbb E\left\lvert t\right\rvert^2. \label{trans:relative} \end{aligned}$$ Here $\mu$ is the conditional single-root closure coefficient of [mat:closure](#mat:closure), [mat:cutoff](#mat:cutoff). The expectation is the branch expectation, and $\mathbb E_B$ averages its boundary law. The hidden root value has already been averaged in $\mu$; averaging it again does not change any other quantity in these formulas.
  - Explanation: Branch transmission has a lower bound relative to boundary response weight. The transmission-weighted closing error is exponentially smaller than the transmission, uniformly over permitted passive exterior data.
- **claim-adaptive-interior-envelope** (bound, [source](#stmt-trans-envelope)): For almost every fixed boundary datum there is a nonnegative function $\mathfrak E(v_V)$ depending only on the true interior potentials, the fixed exterior and boundary data, and the artificial mask, such that every allowed internal configuration satisfies $$\sum_{d\in D}\left\lvert S_{d,\rm source}\right\rvert^2 \le\mathfrak E(v_V),\qquad \mathbb E_{V,\rm true}\mathfrak E\le e^{C(1+L)^C}\rho. \label{trans:envelope-bound}$$ The same integrated bound, with adjusted constants, holds under any density bounded by $e^{C(1+L)^C}$ relative to the true product law on $V$. The constants are uniform in the exterior data and in all adaptive interior choices.
  - Explanation: A single function of the true interior potentials dominates every allowed adaptive internal port configuration. Its expectation is controlled by the normalized boundary response, also under the stated bounded density changes.
- **claim-conditional-acceptance-embeds-branch-law** (construction, [source](#stmt-hier-sampling)): Fix the offset and all selector marks. Expose the box identities of the eligible tiles and of their ancestors. Conditional on this information, the unexplored variables in disjoint eligible tiles are independent. For an allowed eligible tile $U$ of level $l$, its conditional acceptance probability is $p_l$, and conditional on acceptance its site variables have exactly the branch law of [cell:branch](#cell:branch) with the indicated corner conditioning.* *These conclusions remain valid for $U$ after fixing status decisions or other local choices in disjoint eligible tiles, provided the exposed box identities are retained in the conditioning. In particular, $$\mathbb P(A_U=1\mid\text{offset})\le q_l p_l. \label{hier:incidence}$$ The unconditional law of the true potentials remains the iid product law. The same construction can be made prospectively by omitting all selectors and ancestor tests above a specified cutoff level.
  - Explanation: Conditioning on selectors and the prescribed labels preserves independence across eligible tiles. Acceptance realizes the branch law without changing the unconditional true product field.
- **claim-hidden-root-conditioning** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-hier-hidden)): At an accepted root whose cell port is still open, condition on the selectors, the needed corner-box identities, all branch acceptance indicators, and the other true potentials. Do not condition on the numerical value of the root tile’s acceptance uniform. The root’s true value then has the form $$v_0=c(v)+\delta,$$ where $\delta$ has the centered, cutoff Cauchy law of [mat:cutoff](#mat:cutoff), with the scale of that cell port. The open response, all other active references and strengths, and any artificial probe at this root are independent of this hidden draw under the stated conditioning.
  - Explanation: The hidden root retains its cutoff Cauchy law under the permitted conditioning. The open response and other active references do not reveal that draw; exposing the numerical acceptance uniform would invalidate this argument.
- **claim-thinning-energy-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-hier-comparison)): There is a deterministic sequence $\zeta_n\downarrow0$, after replacing it by a decreasing majorant if necessary, such that for all sufficiently large $n$, every $n<m\le N$, every allowed artificial-probe mask, and every pass or prefix just described, $$\mathbb E\mathcal Q_{\rm end}(g) \le \mathbb E\mathcal Q_{\rm start}(g) +C c_g^2\left( \zeta_n\mathbb E\mathcal Q_{\rm start}(f) +\mathcal C_{n,m}(f)\right). \label{hier:comparison-equation}$$ The constants and $\zeta_n$ are independent of $m,N$, the pass region, the mask satisfying [hier:probe-range](#hier:probe-range), and the finite dimensions of the real vector data.
  - Explanation: Every permitted thinning pass or prefix has an expected energy increase controlled by a vanishing multiplicative error and an additive oscillation charge. Adaptive data must preserve each hidden root law.
- **claim-parent-alternative-interpolation-cap** (bound, [source](#stmt-hier-cap)): There are constants $C,C_1$ such that, with $K_l=\exp(C(1+b_{l+1})^{C_1})$, for $0\le\theta\le1$, $$Z_l(\theta) \le\theta K_l\mathbb E\mathcal Q_{\rm current}(f) +C\theta B_l(f). \label{hier:cap-equation}$$ The estimate holds during either pass and during every level interpolation in that pass.
  - Explanation: An active parent alternative bounds exterior scattering from surviving child roots by a quantity proportional to the retention parameter. This removes the apparent singularity at the end of thinning.
- **claim-global-and-local-charge-tails** (bound, [source](#stmt-hier-charges)): Put $r_n=\sum_{l=n}^{\infty}q_lp_l$, so that $r_n\to0$.* 1. *If $f$ has global lattice Lipschitz constant at most $K$, then $$b_N^{-2}\mathcal C_{n,m}(f)\le C K^2 r_n, \qquad n<m\le N.$$* 2. *Suppose $f$ vanishes outside a square of side at most $A R$ and has Lipschitz constant at most $K/R$. If $b_m\le R$ and the square is interpreted in a coordinate neighborhood of the torus, then $$\mathcal C_{n,m}(f)\le C_A K^2 r_n.$$ Both assertions hold for offset-dependent locations and prescriptions, and for real Euclidean-vector data.
  - Explanation: The charge tail vanishes both after area normalization for globally Lipschitz data and without normalization for localized data with the specified support and scale separation.
- **claim-generic-local-observation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-screen-observation)): For sufficiently large $Q_1$, almost every choice of the true potentials on $\Lambda_{3Q_1}(z)$ has the following property. For every set $\mathcal O\subset\Lambda_{3Q_1}(z)$ that is a $C Q_1^{1/4}$-net of $\Lambda_{2Q_1}(z)$, there is a finite constant $C_{\mathrm{obs}}$ such that $$\left\lVert u\|_{\mathrm{core}}\right\rVert \leq C_{\mathrm{obs}} \left(\left\lVert(H-E)u\|_{\Lambda_{3Q_1}(z)}\right\rVert +\left\lVert u\|_{\mathcal O}\right\rVert\right) \label{screen:observation-bound}$$ for every vector on $\Lambda_{3Q_1+1}(z)$. After $Q_1$ is fixed, a deterministic $C_{\mathrm{obs}}$ can be chosen so that this bound holds for every such set $\mathcal O$ outside an event of arbitrarily small probability. The exceptional probability is uniform in the offset and in all sufficiently large tori.
  - Explanation: Almost every local true potential configuration allows residual and observation-net data to control the core. A deterministic high quantile gives a common observation constant outside an arbitrarily small exceptional probability.
- **claim-projected-screening-and-shield-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-screen-projected)): For all the systems just described, $$\left\lVert\Pi^\top G\Pi\right\rVert\leq C_n. \label{screen:projected-bound}$$ Give cost one to nearest-neighbor edges whose endpoints both belong to counted good cores, and cost zero to other edges; any of the positive costs may also be dropped. If two source and response sectors of $\Pi$ have cost distance at least $t$, their resolvent block has norm at most $C_n\exp(-c_nt)$.* *Suppose, further, that two systems agree on an inner region and a surrounding shield, including all ports there, and that counted good tests in the shield are usable in both systems. Count costs only on shared good-good edges in the shield. If every crossing of the shield from the inner region to the exterior costs at least $t$, then the responses between common inner ports differ by at most $C_n\exp(-c_nt)$ in operator norm. This includes comparison with a Dirichlet restriction buffered beyond the shield.
  - Explanation: Intact good-core tests bound only the projected inverse needed for propagation. Good-edge distance gives attenuation and comparison across a shared shield without requiring a bound on the full inverse.
- **claim-full-stage-crossing-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-screen-paths)): There is an absolute $\alpha>0$ such that, for the full stage-$n$ good-core costs and all sufficiently large $r$ up to a small fixed fraction of the torus side, $$\mathbb P\left(\begin{array}{c} \text{some nearest-neighbor crossing from }\Lambda_r(x) \text{ to }\Lambda_{2r}(x)^c\\ \text{has cost less than }c_nr^\alpha \end{array}\,\middle\|\,\text{offset}\right) \leq C_nr^{-50}. \label{screen:path-bound}$$ Only costs of edges with both endpoints in the intervening band need be counted. The constants are uniform in the center and the allowed prescriptions.
  - Explanation: A multiscale independent-pair recursion gives a positive power of crossing cost outside a polynomially small failure event. The estimate counts only the intervening band.
- **claim-retained-wall-crossing-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-screen-walls)): Fix an aligned square grid of side $L_*$, deterministic given the offset. Suppose a system retains the full stage-$n$ data on a wall set containing strips of width $\delta L_*$ along every grid boundary, where $\delta>0$ is a fixed absolute constant. Count only good cores whose centers are at least $5Q_1$ inside this wall set, so that all their tests are intact. There is an absolute constant $A_{\mathrm w}$ such that, for $L_*$ sufficiently large at fixed $n$ and $$A_{\mathrm w}L_*\leq r\leq c b_N,$$ every crossing of the $r$-band has wall cost at least $c_n'r^\alpha$ outside an event of conditional probability at most $C_n'r^{-30}$. Here $c_n',C_n'$ and the threshold on $L_*$ may depend on $n$; $A_{\mathrm w}$ depends only on the absolute strip fraction and geometric constants. In particular, the constants in the bound are uniform as $L_*$ grows. As before, only edges within the crossed band need be counted.
  - Explanation: Thick retained walls provide crossing costs with constants uniform as the grid side grows. Only cores buffered inside the walls and retaining intact tests contribute.
- **claim-fixed-stage-jump-tails-and-spatial-limits** (bound, [source](#stmt-screen-limits)): For each fixed sufficiently large $n$ there is a finite number $d_n\geq0$ with the following properties. The jump tails satisfy $$\sup\mathbb E\left[d_{n,N}^{>D}(x)\mid\text{offset}\right] \leq\omega_n(D),\qquad \omega_n(D)\longrightarrow0, \label{screen:jump-tail}$$ uniformly in the site, sufficiently large tori, prescribed masks, and $0\leq\tau\leq1$. For a deterministic site $x$, with expectation including the uniform offset, $$\mathbb Ed_{n,N}(x)\longrightarrow d_n \qquad (N\longrightarrow\infty,\ \tau\longrightarrow0), \label{screen:point-limit}$$ uniformly in the prescriptions. If $A$ is a growing square, whose position may be chosen from the offset and external independent randomness, then $$\frac1{\|A\|}\sum_{x\in A}d_{n,N}(x)\longrightarrow d_n \quad\text{in mean and in probability}. \label{screen:spatial-limit}$$ The estimates for this convergence are uniform conditional on the offset, over such square positions and prescriptions, as the square side and the torus size grow and $\tau\to0$. Bounded-length jumps capture these spatial averages to any given tolerance with arbitrarily high probability if the jump cutoff is chosen first and sufficiently large.
  - Explanation: At a fixed starting stage, long jumps have a uniformly vanishing energy tail. Local expectations and large-square averages approach a finite deterministic density as volume grows and probes vanish.
- **claim-compact-profile-energy-upper-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-screen-profiles)): Let $\varphi:\mathbb R^2\to\mathbb R^q$ be compactly supported and Lipschitz, piecewise smooth with bounded gradients and piecewise regular interfaces. Let its center $c$ be prescribed from the offset and external independent randomness. At full stage $n$, $$\limsup_{\substack{R\to\infty,\ \tau\to0\\b_N/R\to\infty}} \mathbb E\,\mathcal Q_{\mathrm{start}} \bigl(\varphi((\cdot-c)/R)\bigr) \leq C d_n\int_{\mathbb R^2}\|\nabla\varphi\|^2\,\,\mathrm dx, \label{screen:profile-bound}$$ where $C$ is absolute. Here $n$ and the profile are fixed; the thresholds may depend arbitrarily on them. The estimate is uniform over the allowed masks and centers, and remains valid with the expectation conditioned on the offset.* *More generally, let $f$ be supported in a square of side at most $A R$ whose position is prescribed from the offset and external independent randomness, and suppose that $f$ has global Lipschitz constant at most $B/R$. Then $$\mathbb E\,\mathcal Q_{\mathrm{start}}(f) \leq C_{A,B}(d_n+o(1)) \label{screen:rough-profile}$$ in the same fixed-$n$ regime, uniformly over all such data and prescriptions. Thus each assertion holds to any fixed additive tolerance for all sufficiently large spatial and torus scales and all sufficiently small $\tau$; no rate uniform in $n$ is asserted.
  - Explanation: Macroscopic compact profiles have an energy upper bound proportional to the fixed-stage density and continuum gradient energy. The rough-profile variant is uniform over data satisfying the specified support and Lipschitz bounds; equality or a uniform stage rate is not claimed.
- **claim-convergence-of-affine-energy-densities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-screen-limit)): The fixed-stage limits satisfy $$d_n\longrightarrow d\quad\text{for some }0\leq d<\infty. \label{screen:d-limit}$$ More precisely, a deterministic $\varepsilon_n\to0$ can be chosen such that $$d_m\leq(1+\varepsilon_n)d_n+\varepsilon_n \qquad(m>n) \label{screen:almost-monotone}$$ for all sufficiently large $n$, uniformly in $m$.
  - Explanation: The thinning comparison gives an almost-monotonicity inequality with vanishing errors. Boundedness and a lower-limit subsequence then force convergence of the stage densities.
- **claim-vanishing-affine-energy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-van-zero)): The limiting affine energy vanishes: $d=0$.
  - Explanation: The limiting auxiliary affine energy is zero. The proof uses planar logarithmic capacity and an analytic submean contradiction, rather than a time-evolution assertion.
- **claim-adaptive-first-variation-smallness** (bound, [source](#stmt-van-adjustment)): Fix $M_0$. For every $a>0$, one can arrange $$\label{van:adjustment-small} \mathbb E\bigl[\mathbf 1_{\mathcal P}\|H^0\|\bigr]\leq a$$ by taking $n$, then $m>n$, then $N$ sufficiently large. More precisely, the estimate holds for all sufficiently large $n$, all sufficiently large $m$ depending on $n$, and all sufficiently large $N$ depending on the fixed pair $(n,m)$.
  - Explanation: An admissible signed adjustment of affine data forces the expected local first variation to be arbitrarily small on the selector event. The hierarchy and torus parameters are chosen successively.
- **claim-annular-probe-barrier** (bound, [source](#stmt-van-barrier)): For suitable fixed $d_0,\gamma>0$, $\mathbb P(\mathcal B^c)\leq C e^{-cM_0R}$. On $\mathcal B\cap\mathcal P$, for every compatible matrix $D'$ on the source columns indexed by $J$, $$\label{van:barrier-bound} \left\lVert A_{IJ}D'\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}} \leq \frac{C}{M_0R\tau d_0} \left\lVert S_{CJ}D'\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}.$$ The constant includes the fixed factor $\gamma^{-1}$. The same cutoff argument controls every absorbed output supported where the cutoff equals one; it requires no norm bound on the exterior resolvent.
  - Explanation: A good annular barrier fails with exponentially small probability. On the barrier and selector events, a cutoff bounds interior response by absorbed probe response without controlling the exterior inverse.
- **claim-positive-jump-energy-good-event** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-van-good)): Assume $d>0$. There is a constant $c_1>0$ with the following property. For every sufficiently small fixed $\kappa>0$, one can choose $M_0$ sufficiently large and finite constants $C_1,C_F$, before choosing $C_0,n,m,N$, so that the following holds. For any fixed sufficiently large $C_0$, and then $n,m,N$ sufficiently large successively, an event $\mathcal G$ of probability at least $1/2$ satisfies $$\begin{gathered} \mathcal G\subset\mathcal P\cap\mathcal B,\qquad K\leq\kappa,\label{van:good-transmission}\\ c_1d\leq \sum_{i,j\in I}\|F(i)-F(j)\|^2\|A_{ij}\|^2 \leq C_1,\label{van:good-jumps}\\ \left\lVert F_\nu T\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq C_F \quad (1\leq\nu\leq4).\label{van:good-weighted} \end{gathered}$$ In particular, on this event, $$\label{van:external-small} \left\lVert A_{IJ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}} +\sum_{\nu=1}^4\left\lVert A_{IJ}F_\nu\|_J\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}} \leq \frac{C(\sqrt\kappa+C_F)}{M_0C_0d_0}.$$ The right side tends to zero as $C_0$ increases, with all constants chosen before it kept fixed.
  - Explanation: Assuming a positive limiting density, a good event combines small probe transmission, positive retained internal jump energy and bounded coordinate-weighted transmission. Increasing the later probe constant makes exterior leakage small.
- **claim-analytic-central-value-and-boundary-moments** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-van-analytic-bounds)): Choose $\kappa$ sufficiently small depending on $d$, then the constants in <a href="#van:good" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="van:good">34</a>, and then $C_0$ sufficiently large. There are constants $c_2>0$ and $B_p<\infty$, independent of the later choices $n,m,N$, such that on $\mathcal G$ $$\label{van:central-bound} \|\mathfrak h(0)\|\geq c_2,$$ and, for every fixed $0<p<1/2$, $$\label{van:hardy-bound} \int_{\mathbb T}\|H(z)\|^p\,\,\mathrm d\gamma_{\mathbb T}(z)\leq B_p.$$ Here $\gamma_{\mathbb T}$ is normalized Haar measure. Neither constant depends on the number of cell ports or probes.
  - Explanation: On the good event the analytic trace has a nonzero central value and a bounded fractional boundary moment, with constants independent of port counts. These bounds supply the contradiction against boundary smallness.
- **claim-true-torus-fractional-moment-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-boot-fractional)): Fix $0<s<1/16$. There are constants $A=A(h,E,s)<\infty$, $a=a(h,E,s)>0$, and an integer $N_0=N_0(h,E,s)$ such that, for every $N\ge N_0$ and every $x,y\in\mathbb T_N^2$, $$\label{boot:real-decay} \mathbb E\left\lvert(H_N-E)^{-1}_{xy}\right\rvert^{s/2} \le A\exp\{-a\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,y)\}.$$ Here $H_N$ has the true iid uniform $[-h,h]$ diagonal and periodic nearest-neighbor hopping. Its inverse at the specified real energy exists almost surely. No uniformity in $E$, nor any statement about time evolution, is part of this theorem.
  - Explanation: The true undamped torus Green function has exponentially decaying fractional moments at each fixed interior real energy. Constants depend on disorder, energy and the fractional exponent; time evolution and energy uniformity are excluded.
- **claim-filled-annulus-mixture-domination** (bound, [source](#stmt-boot-twist)): There is $C_s<\infty$, independent of the masks, centers, $R,N$, and $C_0\ge1$, such that $$\label{boot:twist-ineq} \mathbb E\left\lVert S^{\rm old}_{Y'X}\right\rVert_{\rm HS}^{s} \le C_s(1+C_0)\mathbb E\bigl[D^s+V^sU^s\bigr].$$ As everywhere in this section, the constant may depend on the fixed $h,E,s$ and profile.
  - Explanation: A common closing-phase mixture dominates the true product law and bounds old-mask transmission by direct filled-mask transmission and a product of annular transmissions.
- **claim-uniform-transmission-tilted-site-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-boot-tilted)): Suppose $\mathbb EV^s>0$, and define the probability law $$\,\mathrm d\nu=\frac{V^s}{\mathbb EV^s}\,\,\mathrm d\mathbb P.$$ For a site $i\in C$, conditional on all other potentials, put $W(v_i)=V^s$. Then $$\label{boot:l2l1} \bigl(\mathbb E_i W^2\bigr)^{1/2}\le K_s\mathbb E_i W,$$ with a constant uniform in every frozen exterior and in the mask. For $d_0>0$ sufficiently small, chosen after $s$ and before $C_0$, there are $K,c>0$ such that $$\label{boot:tilted-barriers} \mathbb P(\mathcal B^c)+\nu(\mathcal B^c)\le K e^{-cR}, \qquad \mathbb E_\nu d_{\min}^{-2s}\le K R^2, \qquad d_{\min}=\min_{i\in C}\vartheta(v_i).$$ No independence under $\nu$ is asserted or needed.
  - Explanation: Single-site moment comparison controls bad-site events even under a transmission tilt. Iteration gives barrier failure bounds and endpoint-profile integrability without asserting independence under the tilted law.
- **claim-small-filled-annulus-return** (bound, [source](#stmt-boot-return)): For each fixed sufficiently large $C_0$ and each $\epsilon>0$, one can choose $n$, then $m>n$, then a threshold for $N$, so that $$\mathbb EU^2<\epsilon$$ for every subsequent $N$, every deterministic mask, every center, and every permitted subset $X$.
  - Explanation: Vanishing affine energy, thinning comparison and a buffered cutoff make mean-square return to the filled annulus arbitrarily small. Choices are uniform over each allowed deterministic prescription.
- **claim-uniform-mask-transmission-contraction** (bound, [source](#stmt-boot-contraction)): For fixed $h,E,s$, parameters through $R$ and an integer $N_0$ can be chosen so that, for every $N\ge N_0$, $$F_N(r)\le\tfrac12 F_N(r-4R),\qquad r\ge10R.$$ Here $F_N(r)$ is the supremum of $\mathbb E\left\lVert S_{Y'X}\right\rVert_{\rm HS}^s$ over all deterministic artificial masks, all subsets $X,Y'$ lying in radius-$R$ squares, and all centers at distance at least $r$. A supremum over an empty family is zero. Consequently there are constants $K,a_1>0$, independent of $N\ge N_0$, such that $$\label{boot:mask-decay} F_N(r)\le K R^s e^{-a_1 r/R},\qquad r\ge0.$$
  - Explanation: Finite-scale smallness contracts the supremum of transmission moments over all deterministic masks. Iterating the contraction gives exponential spatial decay with constants uniform in the sufficiently large torus.
- **claim-square-summable-column-and-inverse-square-integral** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-column)): For every fixed $h>0$, every fixed $E\in(-4-h,4+h)$ and every site $x\in\mathbb Z^2$, almost surely there is $u\in\ell^2(\mathbb Z^2)$ such that $$(H_v-E)u=\delta_x.$$ If $\mu_x$ is the scalar spectral measure of $\delta_x$, then almost surely $$\label{spec:gamma} \Gamma_x(E):=\int_\mathbb R\frac{\mu_x(\,\mathrm de)}{(e-E)^2}<\infty.$$ The integrand is interpreted as $+\infty$ at $e=E$.
  - Explanation: A configuration-dependent subsequence of finite-torus columns yields a square-summable infinite-volume solution. The spectral theorem then makes the inverse-square spectral integral finite at the fixed energy.
- **claim-rank-one-spectral-averaging** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-averaging)): Let $H_0$ be bounded self-adjoint and let $\xi$ be a unit vector. For $a\in\mathbb R$, let $\mu_a$ be the scalar spectral measure of $\xi$ for $H_0+a\langle\xi,\cdot\rangle\xi$. Then, as measures on $\mathbb R$, $$\label{spec:average} \int_\mathbb R\mu_a\,\mathrm da\le \operatorname{Leb}.$$ Suppose on a Borel set $B$ the base measure $\mu_0$ satisfies $\int(e-E)^{-2}\mu_0(\,\mathrm de)<\infty$ for Lebesgue-almost every $E\in B$. For almost every $a$, the restriction of $\mu_a$ to $B$ is concentrated on eigenvalues of the perturbed operator.
  - Explanation: Averaging rank-one spectral measures is dominated by Lebesgue measure. Finiteness of the base inverse-square integral almost everywhere on a Borel set then concentrates almost every perturbed measure there on eigenvalues.
- **claim-site-measures-give-complete-eigenbasis** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-readout)): For each fixed $h>0$, almost surely every site spectral measure of $H_v$ is pure point. Consequently $H_v$ has pure-point spectral type and a complete orthonormal eigenbasis.
  - Explanation: Measurability, Fubini and single-site spectral averaging give purity of each site measure. Intersecting over sites makes the pure-point subspace the entire Hilbert space without assuming a site vector is globally cyclic.
- **claim-deterministic-spectrum-and-dense-eigenvalues** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-spectrum)): For each fixed $h>0$, almost surely $\sigma(H_v)=[-4-h,4+h]$. Under <a href="#spec:readout" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:readout">43</a>, the eigenvalues are dense in this interval.
  - Explanation: Nearly constant random boxes support approximate free plane waves, filling the deterministic spectral interval. A complete eigenbasis identifies that interval with the closure of the eigenvalues.

For a port of strength $\\sigma$, give its hidden real shift $\\delta$ the law $$\\frac1{Z_\\sigma}\\chi(\\delta)\n       \\frac{\\sigma\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\delta}{\\pi(\\delta^2+\\sigma^2)},\n \\qquad\n Z_\\sigma=\\int_\\mathbb R\\chi(\\delta)\n       \\frac{\\sigma\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\delta}{\\pi(\\delta^2+\\sigma^2)}.\n \\label{mat:cutoff-law}$$ Use $w=\\delta/\\sigma$ in [mat:closure](#mat:closure). Conditional on the open system, let $g=\\sigma G_{xx}$, where $x$ is the site of the port. For all sufficiently small $\\sigma$, the mean in [mat:energy-closure](#mat:energy-closure) satisfies $$\\left\\lvert\\mu\\right\\rvert\\le C,\\qquad\n \\left\\lvert\\mu\\right\\rvert\\le C\\sqrt\\sigma\\quad\\hbox{if }\\left\\lvert g\\right\\rvert>\\sqrt\\sigma.\n \\label{mat:cutoff-estimate}$$ The constants depend on $\\chi$, not on the exterior system.",
          "source_id": "PM-261.2"
        }
      ],

        "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a contraction and let $U,V$ be compatible deterministic matrices.         "anchor": "stmt-mat-phase",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D(s)$ be a continuously differentiable $d\\times d$ matrix on a bounded real interval, with $-\\operatorname{Im}D(s)>0$. Its determinant has a continuous phase $\\Phi$ such that $$-d\\pi<\\Phi(s)<0,\\qquad\n \\Phi'(s)=\\operatorname{Im}\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}\\bigl(D'(s)D(s)^{-1}\\bigr).\n \\label{mat:phase-derivative}$$ Suppose, in particular, that $$D'(s)=P_J-i\\tau\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}_J(a_i'(s)),\\qquad\n 0\\le\\tau\\le1,\\qquad \\left\\lvert a_i'(s)\\right\\rvert^2\\le C a_i(s),$$ where $P_J$ is the coordinate projection onto $J$, and that $-\\operatorname{Im}D(s)\\ge\\tau\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}_J(a_i(s))$. With $G=D^{-1}$, $$\\Phi'(s)\\ge\\tfrac12\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_J\\operatorname{Im}G(s)-C'd.\n \\label{mat:phase-lower}$$ Consequently, on an interval $[a,b]$, $$\\int_a^b\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_J\\operatorname{Im}D(s)^{-1}\\,\\,\\mathrm ds\n                  \\le C''d(1+b-a).\n \\label{mat:phase-integral}$$ The integral bound remains valid for passive matrices at their almost-everywhere existing inverses, by strict regularization.",
        "source_id": "PM-261.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cell-geometry",
        "exact_quote": "The set $Z$ consists of the four sides of the square with their corners removed. Every site of $Z$ has an inward nearest neighbor in $J\\setminus\nZ$. The restriction $M:P\\longrightarrow\\mathbb R^Z$ is an isomorphism whose singular values lie in $[1,\\sqrt3]$. In particular, $$\\left\\lVert M\\right\\rVert\\le\\sqrt3,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert Mw\\right\\rVert\\ge\\left\\lVert w\\right\\rVert\\quad(w\\in P).$$ There is an orthogonal decomposition $P=P_0+Q$: $P_0$ is the coordinate projection onto the single side sites of $B$, and $Q$ is the direct sum of four two-dimensional corner planes.",
        "source_id": "PM-261.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "source_id": "PM-261.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The set $Z$ consists of the four sides of the square with their corners removed. Every site of $Z$ has an inward nearest neighbor in $J\\setminus\nZ$.       "explanation": "Clipping the corners gives an inward neighbor at every coupled boundary site. The boundary coupling is invertible on its row space, with a separate plane for each corner.",

      "hypotheses": [

          "anchor": "cell:sets",
          "exact_quote": "$$L=\\frac{b_l-1}{2}-b,\\qquad\n V=\\{-L,\\ldots,L\\}^2\\setminus\\{(\\pm L,\\pm L)\\},\\qquad\n J=V\\setminus\\{0\\}.\n \\label{cell:sets}$$ Only sufficiently large $l$ are used. Let $$B=\\{x\\notin V:\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_1(x,V)=1\\},\n \\qquad p_x=\\mathbf 1_{\\{|x|_1=1\\}}\\quad(x\\in J).$$ Thus $p$ is the adjacency column from the root $0$ to $J$. Let $M:\\mathbb R^B\\longrightarrow\\mathbb R^J$ be the adjacency matrix from $B$ to $J$; when used on $V$, it has an additional zero row at the root.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cell-profile",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed exponent $C_2$ such that, for every sufficiently large $L$, a deterministic $u_L\\in[-c_u/L,c_u/L]$ can be chosen for which the real symmetric Dirichlet matrix $$A^0=\\operatorname{Adj}_J+\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}_J(v^0-E)$$ satisfies $\\left\\lVert(A^0)^{-1}\\right\\rVert\\le L^{C_2}$. The bounds in [cell:profile-size](#cell:profile-size), [cell:design-margin](#cell:design-margin) hold for this choice, with constants independent of $L$.",
        "source_id": "PM-261.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cell-profile",
          "exact_quote": "There is a fixed exponent $C_2$ such that, for every sufficiently large $L$, a deterministic $u_L\\in[-c_u/L,c_u/L]$ can be chosen for which the real symmetric Dirichlet matrix $$A^0=\\operatorname{Adj}_J+\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}_J(v^0-E)$$ satisfies $\\left\\lVert(A^0)^{-1}\\right\\rVert\\le L^{C_2}$. The bounds in [cell:profile-size](#cell:profile-size), [cell:design-margin](#cell:design-margin) hold for this choice, with constants independent of $L$.",
          "source_id": "PM-261.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a fixed exponent $C_2$ such that, for every sufficiently large $L$, a deterministic $u_L\\in[-c_u/L,c_u/L]$ can be chosen for which the real symmetric Dirichlet matrix $$A^0=\\operatorname{Adj}_J+\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}_J(v^0-E)$$ satisfies $\\left\\lVert(A^0)^{-1}\\right\\rVert\\le L^{C_2}$. The bounds in [cell:profile-size](#cell:profile-size), [cell:design-margin](#cell:design-margin) hold for this choice, with constants independent of $L$.",
      "explanation": "A deterministic profile deformation can avoid the small eigenvalues of the punctured Dirichlet matrix while preserving the designed decay and potential margins.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "cell:profile-formula",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\begin{aligned}\n \\psi_x(u)&=e^{-\\nu|x|_\\infty}\n       \\frac{\\cos(\\theta(x_1+x_2)+\\phi)}{\\cos\\phi}\n       e^{u f_{x_1+x_2}}\\quad(x\\in V\\cup B),\\\\\n v_x^0(u)&=E-\\sum_{y\\sim x}\\frac{\\psi_y(u)}{\\psi_x(u)}\n       \\quad(x\\in V).\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{cell:profile-formula}$$ Every neighbor in the last sum belongs to $V\\cup B$ and $\\psi_0=1$. Uniformly over the allowed parameters, $$c e^{-\\nu|x|_\\infty}\\le |\\psi_x(u)|\n       \\le C e^{-\\nu|x|_\\infty}.\n \\label{cell:profile-size}$$ Indeed $|u f_{x_1+x_2}|$ is bounded by a fixed multiple of $c_u$ and the periodic carrier is bounded away from zero. For neighbors $x,y$ the additional ratio factors differ from $1$ by $O(\\nu+c_u)$: their radial exponents differ by at most $\\nu$, and $|u(f_{y_1+y_2}-f_{x_1+x_2})|\\le Cc_u$. At $\\nu=u=0$, the cosine addition formula gives $v_x^0=E-4\\cos\\theta$. We therefore fix $\\nu,c_u$ small enough, once and for all, that $$|v_x^0(u)-(E-4\\cos\\theta)|\\le d,\n \\qquad |v_x^0(u)|\\le h-7d.\n \\label{cell:design-margin}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-261.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "cell:design-margin",
          "exact_quote": "$$|v_x^0(u)-(E-4\\cos\\theta)|\\le d,\n \\qquad |v_x^0(u)|\\le h-7d.\n \\label{cell:design-margin}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-261.2"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cell-branch",
        "exact_quote": "For sufficiently large $L$, the above choices have the following properties, uniformly over permitted corner boxes:*\n\n\n1.  *Every interior draw has $$\\left\\lVert A^{-1}\\right\\rVert\\le2L^{C_2},\\qquad\n      \\left\\lVert-A^{-1}p-\\psi|_J\\right\\rVert\\le C L^{C_2+1}\\epsilon.\n      \\label{cell:residual}$$ The exponents in these bounds are independent of $D_2$, provided $D_3$ is subsequently chosen sufficiently large.*\n\n2.  *All true potentials on $V$ lie in a fixed compact subinterval of $(-h,h)$. The root center satisfies $$|c(v)-v_0^0|\\le C L^{C_2+1}\\epsilon.\n      \\label{cell:root-calibration}$$ The plateau of $\\vartheta$ can therefore be fixed to contain every true value on $V$ and every single side value in $B$, for all the cells used. The artificial absorptions on these sites are constant as $r$, the jitters, the common side shift, and the hidden root draw vary.*\n\n3.  *Relative to the true product law on the tile conditioned on these same four corner boxes, the branch has a density $\\mathcal L$ satisfying $$0\\le\\mathcal L\\le\n           \\exp\\bigl(C L^3+C L^2\\log L\\bigr),\n      \\qquad \\int\\mathcal L\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\mathbb P_{\\rm true,boxes}=1.\n      \\label{cell:likelihood}$$ The corner-box conditional laws and the laws outside $V\\cup B$ are unchanged.*\n\n*Artificial probes may have any prescribed mask of absent, constant, or profile-dependent strengths, as in [mat:probes](#mat:probes).       "exact_statement": "For sufficiently large $L$, the above choices have the following properties, uniformly over permitted corner boxes:*\n\n\n1.  *Every interior draw has $$\\left\\lVert A^{-1}\\right\\rVert\\le2L^{C_2},\\qquad\n      \\left\\lVert-A^{-1}p-\\psi|_J\\right\\rVert\\le C L^{C_2+1}\\epsilon.\n      \\label{cell:residual}$$ The exponents in these bounds are independent of $D_2$, provided $D_3$ is subsequently chosen sufficiently large.*\n\n2.  *All true potentials on $V$ lie in a fixed compact subinterval of $(-h,h)$. The root center satisfies $$|c(v)-v_0^0|\\le C L^{C_2+1}\\epsilon.\n      \\label{cell:root-calibration}$$ The plateau of $\\vartheta$ can therefore be fixed to contain every true value on $V$ and every single side value in $B$, for all the cells used. The artificial absorptions on these sites are constant as $r$, the jitters, the common side shift, and the hidden root draw vary.*\n\n3.  *Relative to the true product law on the tile conditioned on these same four corner boxes, the branch has a density $\\mathcal L$ satisfying $$0\\le\\mathcal L\\le\n           \\exp\\bigl(C L^3+C L^2\\log L\\bigr),\n      \\qquad \\int\\mathcal L\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\mathbb P_{\\rm true,boxes}=1.\n      \\label{cell:likelihood}$$ The corner-box conditional laws and the laws outside $V\\cup B$ are unchanged.*\n\n*Artificial probes may have any prescribed mask of absent, constant, or profile-dependent strengths, as in [mat:probes](#mat:probes). For subsequent cell estimates their common scale is restricted to $0\\le\\tau\\le e^{-c_*L}$, with a fixed $c_*>2\\nu$.",
      "explanation": "The branch sampler preserves a controlled interior inverse, keeps true cell potentials in a compact interior interval and has a bounded likelihood relative to the conditioned true law. Its internal probe strengths remain constant during the relevant sampling.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cell-branch",
          "exact_quote": "For sufficiently large $L$,",
          "source_id": "PM-261.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "cell:interior-sampler",
          "exact_quote": "$$v_x=v_x^0+r\\mathbf 1_{Z^+}(x)+\\zeta_x,\n \\qquad r\\sim\\operatorname{Unif}[-L^{-D_1},L^{-D_1}],\n \\label{cell:interior-sampler}$$ where the $\\zeta_x$ are independent centered uniforms of half-width $$w_x=\n \\begin{cases}\n L^{-D_3},& L-|x|_\\infty\\le D_2\\log L+5,\\\\\n \\epsilon L^{-D_3},& L-|x|_\\infty>D_2\\log L+5.\n \\end{cases}\n \\label{cell:jitter-widths}$$ They are independent of $r$ and all boundary sampling.",
          "source_id": "PM-261.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "cell:hidden-root",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\begin{gathered}\n v_0=c(v)+\\delta,\\qquad\n \\mathbb P(\\delta\\in\\,\\mathrm dt\\mid v|_J)\n   =\\frac{1}{Z_\\sigma}\\chi(t)\\frac{\\sigma}{\\pi(t^2+\\sigma^2)}\\,\\,\\mathrm dt,\\\\\n Z_\\sigma=\\int_{\\mathbb R}\\chi(t)\\frac{\\sigma}{\\pi(t^2+\\sigma^2)}\\,\\,\\mathrm dt.\n \\end{gathered}\n \\label{cell:hidden-root}$$ This is exactly the cutoff Cauchy law used in [mat:cutoff](#mat:cutoff). The open reference $c(v)$ does not depend on the hidden draw $\\delta$.",
          "source_id": "PM-261.2"
        }
      ],

        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be any fixed complex symmetric matrix on the boundary space with $-\\operatorname{Im}T\\ge0$, and let $k$ be any fixed vector there. For each boundary site prescribe $a_i$ to be one of $0$, $1$, and $\\vartheta$, with the prescription independent of the boundary variables. Set $$\\mathcal T=T+\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}_B\\bigl(v_i-i\\tau a_i(v_i)\\bigr),\n \\qquad\n \\rho=\\left\\lVert P(\\mathcal T-iP)^{-1}k\\right\\rVert^2.\n \\label{imp:rho}$$ Fix $c_*>0$ and suppose $0\\le\\tau\\le e^{-c_*L}$. There are constants $c>0$, $C<\\infty$, and a sufficiently large lower threshold for $L$ such that $$\\mathbb E_B\\!\\left[\n   \\rho\\,\\mathbf 1_{\\{\\left\\lVert P\\mathcal T^{-1}P\\right\\rVert\\le C(1+L)^C\\}}\n \\right]\n \\ge c\\,\\mathbb E_B\\rho.\n \\label{imp:weighted-estimate}$$ Here the expectation uses the boundary law conditional on its four corner boxes. The constants are uniform in all the exterior data just described. Moreover, changing only the single side variables to any other bounded allowed settings, including their artificial absorptions, changes $\\rho$ by at most fixed multiplicative factors, pointwise wherever the inverses exist.",
        "source_id": "PM-261.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "On a rectangular box in a fixed number of real variables, let $p$ be a polynomial of degree at most $d$ with values in a finite-dimensional Hilbert space.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-imp-resolution",
        "exact_quote": "Let $I$ be an interval of length at least $\\ell$, let $N$ be a Hilbert-space-valued polynomial of fixed degree on $I$, and let $z$ be a continuously differentiable complex function satisfying $$\\left\\lvert z(s)\\right\\rvert\\ge c_0 a,\n \\qquad \\left\\lvert z'(s)\\right\\rvert\\le C_0,\n \\qquad a>0.$$ Then $$\\sup_{s\\in I}\\frac{\\left\\lVert N(s)\\right\\rVert^2}{\\left\\lvert z(s)\\right\\rvert^2}\n \\le \\frac{C}{\\min(\\ell,a)}\n       \\int_I\\frac{\\left\\lVert N(s)\\right\\rVert^2}{\\left\\lvert z(s)\\right\\rvert^2}\\,\\,\\mathrm ds.$$ Here $C$ depends only on $c_0,C_0$ and the degree bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-261.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-imp-resolution",
          "exact_quote": "Let $I$ be an interval of length at least $\\ell$, let $N$ be a Hilbert-space-valued polynomial of fixed degree on $I$, and let $z$ be a continuously differentiable complex function satisfying $$\\left\\lvert z(s)\\right\\rvert\\ge c_0 a,\n \\qquad \\left\\lvert z'(s)\\right\\rvert\\le C_0,\n \\qquad a>0.$$ Then $$\\sup_{s\\in I}\\frac{\\left\\lVert N(s)\\right\\rVert^2}{\\left\\lvert z(s)\\right\\rvert^2}\n \\le \\frac{C}{\\min(\\ell,a)}\n       \\int_I\\frac{\\left\\lVert N(s)\\right\\rVert^2}{\\left\\lvert z(s)\\right\\rvert^2}\\,\\,\\mathrm ds.$$ Here $C$ depends only on $c_0,C_0$ and the degree bound.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-imp-schur-estimates",
        "exact_quote": "Uniformly throughout the box, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\operatorname{Im}e&\\ge\\left\\lVert d_1\\right\\rVert^2,\n &\\left\\lvert\\partial_s e\\right\\rvert&\\le C\\tau(1+\\left\\lVert d_1\\right\\rVert),\n \\label{imp:derivatives-a}\\\\\n \\left\\lVert\\partial_s d_1\\right\\rVert&\\le C\\tau,\n &\\left\\lvert\\partial_s\\beta\\right\\rvert&\\le C\\tau\\left\\lVert a\\right\\rVert,\n \\label{imp:derivatives-b}\\\\\n \\left\\lVert\\partial_{t_j}d_1\\right\\rVert&\\le C,\n &\\left\\lvert\\partial_{t_j}e\\right\\rvert&\\le C(1+\\left\\lVert d_1\\right\\rVert).\n \\label{imp:derivatives-c}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If at least one artificial probe is present in the tested triple and $\\tau>0$, then also $$\\operatorname{Im}e\\ge\\frac{\\tau}{\\operatorname{poly}(L)}.\n \\label{imp:probe-floor}$$ For every fixed $t$, $$\\int\\frac{\\left\\lVert d_1(s,t)\\right\\rVert^2}{\\left\\lvert s-e(s,t)\\right\\rvert^2}\\,\\,\\mathrm ds\n \\le\\int\\operatorname{Im}\\frac1{s-e(s,t)}\\,\\,\\mathrm ds\n \\le\\operatorname{poly}(L).\n \\label{imp:s-integral}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-261.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-imp-schur-estimates",
          "exact_quote": "Uniformly throughout the box, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\operatorname{Im}e&\\ge\\left\\lVert d_1\\right\\rVert^2,\n &\\left\\lvert\\partial_s e\\right\\rvert&\\le C\\tau(1+\\left\\lVert d_1\\right\\rVert),\n \\label{imp:derivatives-a}\\\\\n \\left\\lVert\\partial_s d_1\\right\\rVert&\\le C\\tau,\n &\\left\\lvert\\partial_s\\beta\\right\\rvert&\\le C\\tau\\left\\lVert a\\right\\rVert,\n \\label{imp:derivatives-b}\\\\\n \\left\\lVert\\partial_{t_j}d_1\\right\\rVert&\\le C,\n &\\left\\lvert\\partial_{t_j}e\\right\\rvert&\\le C(1+\\left\\lVert d_1\\right\\rVert).\n \\label{imp:derivatives-c}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If at least one artificial probe is present in the tested triple and $\\tau>0$, then also $$\\operatorname{Im}e\\ge\\frac{\\tau}{\\operatorname{poly}(L)}.\n \\label{imp:probe-floor}$$ For every fixed $t$, $$\\int\\frac{\\left\\lVert d_1(s,t)\\right\\rVert^2}{\\left\\lvert s-e(s,t)\\right\\rvert^2}\\,\\,\\mathrm ds\n \\le\\int\\operatorname{Im}\\frac1{s-e(s,t)}\\,\\,\\mathrm ds\n \\le\\operatorname{poly}(L).\n \\label{imp:s-integral}$$",

      "exact_statement": "Uniformly throughout the box, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\operatorname{Im}e&\\ge\\left\\lVert d_1\\right\\rVert^2,\n &\\left\\lvert\\partial_s e\\right\\rvert&\\le C\\tau(1+\\left\\lVert d_1\\right\\rVert),\n \\label{imp:derivatives-a}\\\\\n \\left\\lVert\\partial_s d_1\\right\\rVert&\\le C\\tau,\n &\\left\\lvert\\partial_s\\beta\\right\\rvert&\\le C\\tau\\left\\lVert a\\right\\rVert,\n \\label{imp:derivatives-b}\\\\\n \\left\\lVert\\partial_{t_j}d_1\\right\\rVert&\\le C,\n &\\left\\lvert\\partial_{t_j}e\\right\\rvert&\\le C(1+\\left\\lVert d_1\\right\\rVert).\n \\label{imp:derivatives-c}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If at least one artificial probe is present in the tested triple and $\\tau>0$, then also $$\\operatorname{Im}e\\ge\\frac{\\tau}{\\operatorname{poly}(L)}.\n \\label{imp:probe-floor}$$ For every fixed $t$, $$\\int\\frac{\\left\\lVert d_1(s,t)\\right\\rVert^2}{\\left\\lvert s-e(s,t)\\right\\rvert^2}\\,\\,\\mathrm ds\n \\le\\int\\operatorname{Im}\\frac1{s-e(s,t)}\\,\\,\\mathrm ds\n \\le\\operatorname{poly}(L).\n \\label{imp:s-integral}$$",
      "explanation": "Absorption links the Schur denominator width to the projected coupling. Supported derivatives give uniform variation bounds, and an active artificial probe supplies an additional absorption floor.",

          "anchor": "imp:p1",
          "exact_quote": "Delete the tested missing direction $n$, and denote the inverse of the remaining block of $\\widetilde D$ by $G_{\\mathrm a}$. This inverse is independent of $(s,t)$. The off-diagonal column from $n$ and its Schur denominator have the form $$\\begin{aligned}\n p_1(s,t)&=p_{10}+t_1r_1+t_2r_2\n       -i\\tau\\sum_{i\\text{ in the triple}}a_i(v_i)n_iQe_i,\n \\label{imp:p1}\\\\\n s-e(s,t)&=s-e_{\\mathrm{fix}}\n       -p_1^\\top G_{\\mathrm a}p_1\n       -i\\tau\\sum_{i\\text{ in the triple}}a_i(v_i)n_i^2.\n \\label{imp:denominator}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The fixed vector $p_{10}$ may have components outside the tested triple; no bound on it is assumed. All variable parts of $p_1$ lie in the tested plane. The constants in <a href=\"#imp:denominator\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"imp:denominator\">[imp:denominator]</a> include the fixed diagonal at $n$; there is no real $t$-dependent contribution to that diagonal.",

          "anchor": "imp:W",

The scalar Schur denominator $s-e$ controls how sharply $W$ can concentrate. It need not agree with the corresponding denominator in the actual test $G_\\eta$. Each profile lies on one fixed polynomial piece throughout the corner box. Hence $p_1,d_1,\\beta,e,N_1$ are polynomials in $(s,t)$ of bounded degree, independently of all exterior data.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-imp-schur-interface",
        "exact_quote": "Let $N=\\mathbf 1-P$ on the boundary space. Almost everywhere in the boundary law, the $N,N$ block of $\\mathcal T$ is invertible. Define its Schur matrix and transformed source by $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\widehat T\n &=P\\mathcal TP-P\\mathcal TN(N\\mathcal TN)^{-1}N\\mathcal TP,\\\\\n \\widehat k\n &=Pk-P\\mathcal TN(N\\mathcal TN)^{-1}Nk.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Then $\\widehat T$ is complex symmetric and passive, and $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\widehat T^{-1}&=P\\mathcal T^{-1}P,\\\\\n \\widehat k&=\\widehat T P\\mathcal T^{-1}k,\\\\\n P(\\mathcal T-iP)^{-1}k&=(\\widehat T-i\\mathbf 1_P)^{-1}\\widehat k.\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular $\\rho=\\left\\lVert(\\widehat T-i\\mathbf 1_P)^{-1}\\widehat k\\right\\rVert^2$, and <a href=\"#imp:weighted-estimate\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"imp:weighted-estimate\">[imp:weighted-estimate]</a> controls $\\widehat T^{-1}$ on a set carrying a fixed fraction of this weight.",
        "source_id": "PM-261.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-imp-schur-interface",

        "anchor": "stmt-trans-cell",
        "exact_quote": "Choose the branch parameters in the order described below and fix $c_*>2\\nu$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-hier-sampling",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the offset and all selector marks. Expose the box identities of the eligible tiles and of their ancestors. Conditional on this information, the unexplored variables in disjoint eligible tiles are independent. For an allowed eligible tile $U$ of level $l$, its conditional acceptance probability is $p_l$, and conditional on acceptance its site variables have exactly the branch law of [cell:branch](#cell:branch) with the indicated corner conditioning.*\n\n*These conclusions remain valid for $U$ after fixing status decisions or other local choices in disjoint eligible tiles, provided the exposed box identities are retained in the conditioning. In particular, $$\\mathbb P(A_U=1\\mid\\text{offset})\\le q_l p_l.\n \\label{hier:incidence}$$ The unconditional law of the true potentials remains the iid product law. The same construction can be made prospectively by omitting all selectors and ancestor tests above a specified cutoff level.",
        "source_id": "PM-261.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-hier-hidden",
        "exact_quote": "At an accepted root whose cell port is still open, condition on the selectors, the needed corner-box identities, all branch acceptance indicators, and the other true potentials. Do not condition on the numerical value of the root tile’s acceptance uniform. The root’s true value then has the form $$v_0=c(v)+\\delta,$$ where $\\delta$ has the centered, cutoff Cauchy law of [mat:cutoff](#mat:cutoff), with the scale of that cell port. The open response, all other active references and strengths, and any artificial probe at this root are independent of this hidden draw under the stated conditioning.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-hier-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "There is a deterministic sequence $\\zeta_n\\downarrow0$, after replacing it by a decreasing majorant if necessary, such that for all sufficiently large $n$, every $n<m\\le N$, every allowed artificial-probe mask, and every pass or prefix just described, $$\\mathbb E\\mathcal Q_{\\rm end}(g)\n \\le \\mathbb E\\mathcal Q_{\\rm start}(g)\n       +C c_g^2\\left(\n          \\zeta_n\\mathbb E\\mathcal Q_{\\rm start}(f)\n                    +\\mathcal C_{n,m}(f)\\right).\n \\label{hier:comparison-equation}$$ The constants and $\\zeta_n$ are independent of $m,N$, the pass region, the mask satisfying [hier:probe-range](#hier:probe-range), and the finite dimensions of the real vector data.",
        "source_id": "PM-261.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-hier-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "There is a deterministic sequence $\\zeta_n\\downarrow0$, after replacing it by a decreasing majorant if necessary, such that for all sufficiently large $n$, every $n<m\\le N$, every allowed artificial-probe mask, and every pass or prefix just described, $$\\mathbb E\\mathcal Q_{\\rm end}(g)\n \\le \\mathbb E\\mathcal Q_{\\rm start}(g)\n       +C c_g^2\\left(\n          \\zeta_n\\mathbb E\\mathcal Q_{\\rm start}(f)\n                    +\\mathcal C_{n,m}(f)\\right).\n \\label{hier:comparison-equation}$$ The constants and $\\zeta_n$ are independent of $m,N$, the pass region, the mask satisfying [hier:probe-range](#hier:probe-range), and the finite dimensions of the real vector data.",
          "source_id": "PM-261.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a deterministic sequence $\\zeta_n\\downarrow0$, after replacing it by a decreasing majorant if necessary, such that for all sufficiently large $n$, every $n<m\\le N$, every allowed artificial-probe mask, and every pass or prefix just described, $$\\mathbb E\\mathcal Q_{\\rm end}(g)\n \\le \\mathbb E\\mathcal Q_{\\rm start}(g)\n       +C c_g^2\\left(\n          \\zeta_n\\mathbb E\\mathcal Q_{\\rm start}(f)\n                    +\\mathcal C_{n,m}(f)\\right).\n \\label{hier:comparison-equation}$$ The constants and $\\zeta_n$ are independent of $m,N$, the pass region, the mask satisfying [hier:probe-range](#hier:probe-range), and the finite dimensions of the real vector data.",
      "explanation": "Every permitted thinning pass or prefix has an expected energy increase controlled by a vanishing multiplicative error and an additive oscillation charge. Adaptive data must preserve each hidden root law.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "hier:data-control",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\left\\lvert g_i-g_j\\right\\rvert^2\\le c_g^2\\left\\lvert f_i-f_j\\right\\rvert^2\n                    \\quad\\text{for all sites }i,j.\n \\label{hier:data-control}$$ We permit $g$ to depend on selectors, branch statuses, and the start-of-pass response, and on external independent randomness.         "anchor": "stmt-hier-charges",
        "exact_quote": "Put $r_n=\\sum_{l=n}^{\\infty}q_lp_l$, so that $r_n\\to0$.*\n\n1.  *If $f$ has global lattice Lipschitz constant at most $K$, then $$b_N^{-2}\\mathcal C_{n,m}(f)\\le C K^2 r_n,\n                             \\qquad n<m\\le N.$$*\n\n2.  *Suppose $f$ vanishes outside a square of side at most $A R$ and has Lipschitz constant at most $K/R$.         "anchor": "stmt-screen-observation",
        "exact_quote": "For sufficiently large $Q_1$, almost every choice of the true potentials on $\\Lambda_{3Q_1}(z)$ has the following property. For every set $\\mathcal O\\subset\\Lambda_{3Q_1}(z)$ that is a $C Q_1^{1/4}$-net of $\\Lambda_{2Q_1}(z)$, there is a finite constant $C_{\\mathrm{obs}}$ such that $$\\left\\lVert u|_{\\mathrm{core}}\\right\\rVert\n \\leq C_{\\mathrm{obs}}\n \\left(\\left\\lVert(H-E)u|_{\\Lambda_{3Q_1}(z)}\\right\\rVert\n             +\\left\\lVert u|_{\\mathcal O}\\right\\rVert\\right)\n \\label{screen:observation-bound}$$ for every vector on $\\Lambda_{3Q_1+1}(z)$. After $Q_1$ is fixed, a deterministic $C_{\\mathrm{obs}}$ can be chosen so that this bound holds for every such set $\\mathcal O$ outside an event of arbitrarily small probability.         "anchor": "stmt-screen-projected",
        "exact_quote": "For all the systems just described, $$\\left\\lVert\\Pi^\\top G\\Pi\\right\\rVert\\leq C_n.\n \\label{screen:projected-bound}$$ Give cost one to nearest-neighbor edges whose endpoints both belong to counted good cores, and cost zero to other edges; any of the positive costs may also be dropped. If two source and response sectors of $\\Pi$ have cost distance at least $t$, their resolvent block has norm at most $C_n\\exp(-c_nt)$.*\n\n*Suppose, further, that two systems agree on an inner region and a surrounding shield, including all ports there, and that counted good tests in the shield are usable in both systems. Count costs only on shared good-good edges in the shield. If every crossing of the shield from the inner region to the exterior costs at least $t$, then the responses between common inner ports differ by at most $C_n\\exp(-c_nt)$ in operator norm. This includes comparison with a Dirichlet restriction buffered beyond the shield.",
        "source_id": "PM-261.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-screen-walls",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an aligned square grid of side $L_*$, deterministic given the offset. Suppose a system retains the full stage-$n$ data on a wall set containing strips of width $\\delta L_*$ along every grid boundary, where $\\delta>0$ is a fixed absolute constant. Count only good cores whose centers are at least $5Q_1$ inside this wall set, so that all their tests are intact. There is an absolute constant $A_{\\mathrm w}$ such that, for $L_*$ sufficiently large at fixed $n$ and $$A_{\\mathrm w}L_*\\leq r\\leq c b_N,$$ every crossing of the $r$-band has wall cost at least $c_n'r^\\alpha$ outside an event of conditional probability at most $C_n'r^{-30}$. Here $c_n',C_n'$ and the threshold on $L_*$ may depend on $n$; $A_{\\mathrm w}$ depends only on the absolute strip fraction and geometric constants. In particular, the constants in the bound are uniform as $L_*$ grows. As before, only edges within the crossed band need be counted.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-screen-limits",
        "exact_quote": "For each fixed sufficiently large $n$ there is a finite number $d_n\\geq0$ with the following properties. The jump tails satisfy $$\\sup\\mathbb E\\left[d_{n,N}^{>D}(x)\\mid\\text{offset}\\right]\n       \\leq\\omega_n(D),\\qquad \\omega_n(D)\\longrightarrow0,\n \\label{screen:jump-tail}$$ uniformly in the site, sufficiently large tori, prescribed masks, and $0\\leq\\tau\\leq1$. For a deterministic site $x$, with expectation including the uniform offset, $$\\mathbb Ed_{n,N}(x)\\longrightarrow d_n\n       \\qquad (N\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\ \\tau\\longrightarrow0),\n \\label{screen:point-limit}$$ uniformly in the prescriptions. If $A$ is a growing square, whose position may be chosen from the offset and external independent randomness, then $$\\frac1{|A|}\\sum_{x\\in A}d_{n,N}(x)\\longrightarrow d_n\n       \\quad\\text{in mean and in probability}.\n \\label{screen:spatial-limit}$$ The estimates for this convergence are uniform conditional on the offset, over such square positions and prescriptions, as the square side and the torus size grow and $\\tau\\to0$. Bounded-length jumps capture these spatial averages to any given tolerance with arbitrarily high probability if the jump cutoff is chosen first and sufficiently large.",
        "source_id": "PM-261.2"
      },

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-screen-limits",
          "exact_quote": "For each fixed sufficiently large $n$ there is a finite number $d_n\\geq0$ with the following properties. For a deterministic site $x$, with expectation including the uniform offset, $$\\mathbb Ed_{n,N}(x)\\longrightarrow d_n\n       \\qquad (N\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\ \\tau\\longrightarrow0),\n \\label{screen:point-limit}$$ uniformly in the prescriptions. If $A$ is a growing square, whose position may be chosen from the offset and external independent randomness, then $$\\frac1{|A|}\\sum_{x\\in A}d_{n,N}(x)\\longrightarrow d_n\n       \\quad\\text{in mean and in probability}.\n \\label{screen:spatial-limit}$$ The estimates for this convergence are uniform conditional on the offset, over such square positions and prescriptions, as the square side and the torus size grow and $\\tau\\to0$. Bounded-length jumps capture these spatial averages to any given tolerance with arbitrarily high probability if the jump cutoff is chosen first and sufficiently large.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-screen-profiles",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\varphi:\\mathbb R^2\\to\\mathbb R^q$ be compactly supported and Lipschitz, piecewise smooth with bounded gradients and piecewise regular interfaces. Let its center $c$ be prescribed from the offset and external independent randomness. At full stage $n$, $$\\limsup_{\\substack{R\\to\\infty,\\ \\tau\\to0\\\\b_N/R\\to\\infty}}\n \\mathbb E\\,\\mathcal Q_{\\mathrm{start}}\n       \\bigl(\\varphi((\\cdot-c)/R)\\bigr)\n \\leq C d_n\\int_{\\mathbb R^2}|\\nabla\\varphi|^2\\,\\,\\mathrm dx,\n \\label{screen:profile-bound}$$ where $C$ is absolute. Here $n$ and the profile are fixed; the thresholds may depend arbitrarily on them. The estimate is uniform over the allowed masks and centers, and remains valid with the expectation conditioned on the offset.*\n\n*More generally, let $f$ be supported in a square of side at most $A R$ whose position is prescribed from the offset and external independent randomness, and suppose that $f$ has global Lipschitz constant at most $B/R$. Then $$\\mathbb E\\,\\mathcal Q_{\\mathrm{start}}(f)\n       \\leq C_{A,B}(d_n+o(1))\n \\label{screen:rough-profile}$$ in the same fixed-$n$ regime, uniformly over all such data and prescriptions. Thus each assertion holds to any fixed additive tolerance for all sufficiently large spatial and torus scales and all sufficiently small $\\tau$; no rate uniform in $n$ is asserted.",
        "source_id": "PM-261.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-screen-profiles",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\varphi:\\mathbb R^2\\to\\mathbb R^q$ be compactly supported and Lipschitz, piecewise smooth with bounded gradients and piecewise regular interfaces. Let its center $c$ be prescribed from the offset and external independent randomness. At full stage $n$, $$\\limsup_{\\substack{R\\to\\infty,\\ \\tau\\to0\\\\b_N/R\\to\\infty}}\n \\mathbb E\\,\\mathcal Q_{\\mathrm{start}}\n       \\bigl(\\varphi((\\cdot-c)/R)\\bigr)\n \\leq C d_n\\int_{\\mathbb R^2}|\\nabla\\varphi|^2\\,\\,\\mathrm dx,\n \\label{screen:profile-bound}$$ where $C$ is absolute. Here $n$ and the profile are fixed; the thresholds may depend arbitrarily on them. The estimate is uniform over the allowed masks and centers, and remains valid with the expectation conditioned on the offset.*\n\n*More generally, let $f$ be supported in a square of side at most $A R$ whose position is prescribed from the offset and external independent randomness, and suppose that $f$ has global Lipschitz constant at most $B/R$. Then $$\\mathbb E\\,\\mathcal Q_{\\mathrm{start}}(f)\n       \\leq C_{A,B}(d_n+o(1))\n \\label{screen:rough-profile}$$ in the same fixed-$n$ regime, uniformly over all such data and prescriptions. Thus each assertion holds to any fixed additive tolerance for all sufficiently large spatial and torus scales and all sufficiently small $\\tau$; no rate uniform in $n$ is asserted.",

        "anchor": "stmt-van-good",
        "exact_quote": "Assume $d>0$. There is a constant $c_1>0$ with the following property. For every sufficiently small fixed $\\kappa>0$, one can choose $M_0$ sufficiently large and finite constants $C_1,C_F$, before choosing $C_0,n,m,N$, so that the following holds. For any fixed sufficiently large $C_0$, and then $n,m,N$ sufficiently large successively, an event $\\mathcal G$ of probability at least $1/2$ satisfies $$\\begin{gathered}\n \\mathcal G\\subset\\mathcal P\\cap\\mathcal B,\\qquad\n K\\leq\\kappa,\\label{van:good-transmission}\\\\\n c_1d\\leq\n \\sum_{i,j\\in I}|F(i)-F(j)|^2|A_{ij}|^2\n \\leq C_1,\\label{van:good-jumps}\\\\\n \\left\\lVert F_\\nu T\\right\\rVert_{\\mathrm{HS}}\\leq C_F\n \\quad (1\\leq\\nu\\leq4).\\label{van:good-weighted}\n\\end{gathered}$$ In particular, on this event, $$\\label{van:external-small}\n \\left\\lVert A_{IJ}\\right\\rVert_{\\mathrm{HS}}\n +\\sum_{\\nu=1}^4\\left\\lVert A_{IJ}F_\\nu|_J\\right\\rVert_{\\mathrm{HS}}\n \\leq \\frac{C(\\sqrt\\kappa+C_F)}{M_0C_0d_0}.$$ The right side tends to zero as $C_0$ increases, with all constants chosen before it kept fixed.",
        "source_id": "PM-261.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-boot-fractional",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<s<1/16$. There are constants $A=A(h,E,s)<\\infty$, $a=a(h,E,s)>0$, and an integer $N_0=N_0(h,E,s)$ such that, for every $N\\ge N_0$ and every $x,y\\in\\mathbb T_N^2$, $$\\label{boot:real-decay}\n \\mathbb E\\left\\lvert(H_N-E)^{-1}_{xy}\\right\\rvert^{s/2}\n       \\le A\\exp\\{-a\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,y)\\}.$$ Here $H_N$ has the true iid uniform $[-h,h]$ diagonal and periodic nearest-neighbor hopping.
