<!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Pure-point spectral type** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Pure-point spectral type means that the closed span of the eigenvectors is the entire Hilbert space; it does not mean that every point of the spectrum is an eigenvalue.
- **True uniform product field** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Let $h>0$ and let $(v_x)_{x\in\mathbb Z^2}$ be independent random variables, each uniform on $[-h,h]$.
- **Nearest-neighbor Anderson operator** ([source](#setup:model)): $$\label{setup:model} (H_v u)(x)=\sum_{\left\lvert y-x\right\rvert_1=1}u(y)+v_xu(x) \qquad\text{on }\ell^2(\mathbb Z^2).$$ Thus $v_x=h\omega_x$ in the usual notation with $\omega_x$ uniform on $[-1,1]$. Pure-point spectral type means that the closed span of the eigenvectors is the entire Hilbert space; it does not mean that every point of the spectrum is an eigenvalue.
- **Finite square torus** ([source](#setup:conventions)): The global finite volume is the square torus $$\mathbb T_N^2=(\mathbb Z/b_N\mathbb Z)^2,$$ with the periodic nearest-neighbor adjacency and independent uniform potentials on its sites.
- **Dirichlet restriction** ([source](#setup:conventions)): Dirichlet restrictions mean deletion of the hopping bonds leaving the indicated physical site set.
- **Port column** ([source](#mat:section)): A port at a physical site $x$, of strength $\sigma>0$, is represented by the column $\sqrt{\sigma}\,e_x$.
- **Absorbed response and scattering matrix** ([source](#mat:response)): $$\mathscr L=H^\#-E-iYY^\top,\qquad G=\mathscr L^{-1},\qquad S=\mathbf 1+2iY^\top GY. \label{mat:response}$$ The entry $S_{ja}$ is the scattering amplitude from input port $a$ to output port $j$. The physical matrix $H^\#$ may differ from the true Hamiltonian by real diagonal changes at sites carrying ports. We call these changes *reference potentials*. They may depend on the true potentials.
- **Port Dirichlet energy and single-port contribution** ([source](#mat:energies)): $$\mathcal Q_S(f)=\frac12\sum_{a,j}\left\lvert S_{ja}\right\rvert^2\left\lvert f_j-f_a\right\rvert^2, \qquad \mathcal M_a(f)=\sum_j\left\lvert S_{ja}\right\rvert^2\left\lvert f_j-f_a\right\rvert^2. \label{mat:energies}$$ For scalar data, writing $F=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(f)$, unitarity gives $$\mathcal Q_S(f)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(F^2-FSFS^*). \label{mat:energy-trace}$$ For vector data, one sums this identity over real components.
- **Clipped cell and punctured interior** ([source](#cell:sets)): $$L=\frac{b_l-1}{2}-b,\qquad V=\{-L,\ldots,L\}^2\setminus\{(\pm L,\pm L)\},\qquad J=V\setminus\{0\}. \label{cell:sets}$$ Only sufficiently large $l$ are used. Let $$B=\{x\notin V:\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_1(x,V)=1\}, \qquad p_x=\mathbf 1_{\{\|x\|_1=1\}}\quad(x\in J).$$ Thus $p$ is the adjacency column from the root $0$ to $J$. Let $M:\mathbb R^B\longrightarrow\mathbb R^J$ be the adjacency matrix from $B$ to $J$; when used on $V$, it has an additional zero row at the root. Define $$Z=\{x\in J:M_{x,\cdot}\ne0\},\qquad Z^+=\{x\in J:\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_1(x,Z)\le1\},\qquad P=\operatorname{proj}_{(\ker M)^\perp}. \label{cell:boundary-spaces}$$ We identify a projection with its range when specifying a matrix domain. The cell and its corner coupling are shown in <a href="#fig:clipped-cell" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:clipped-cell">1</a>.
- **Coupled boundary spaces** ([source](#cell:boundary-spaces)): $$Z=\{x\in J:M_{x,\cdot}\ne0\},\qquad Z^+=\{x\in J:\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_1(x,Z)\le1\},\qquad P=\operatorname{proj}_{(\ker M)^\perp}. \label{cell:boundary-spaces}$$ We identify a projection with its range when specifying a matrix domain. The cell and its corner coupling are shown in <a href="#fig:clipped-cell" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:clipped-cell">1</a>.
- **Calibrated root potential** ([source](#cell:calibrated-root)): Whenever $A$ is invertible, define $$c(v)=E+p^\top A^{-1}p. \label{cell:calibrated-root}$$
- **Boundary profile scale and root strength** ([source](#cell:epsilon)): $$\epsilon=e^{-\nu L},\qquad \sigma=\epsilon^2. \label{cell:epsilon}$$ In particular, $\|\psi_x\|$ is comparable to $\epsilon$ on $B$ and on $Z^+$, with constants independent of $L$. We have chosen the port strength $\sigma$ as the square of this designed boundary-profile scale. The exact calibrated mode will satisfy the polynomially lossy comparison in <a href="#cell:residual" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cell:residual">[cell:residual]</a>.
- **Response weight for projected impedance** ([source](#imp:rho)): Let $T$ be any fixed complex symmetric matrix on the boundary space with $-\operatorname{Im}T\ge0$, and let $k$ be any fixed vector there. For each boundary site prescribe $a_i$ to be one of $0$, $1$, and $\vartheta$, with the prescription independent of the boundary variables. Set $$\mathcal T=T+\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}_B\bigl(v_i-i\tau a_i(v_i)\bigr), \qquad \rho=\left\lVert P(\mathcal T-iP)^{-1}k\right\rVert^2. \label{imp:rho}$$ Fix $c_*>0$ and suppose $0\le\tau\le e^{-c_*L}$. There are constants $c>0$, $C<\infty$, and a sufficiently large lower threshold for $L$ such that $$\mathbb E_B\!\left[ \rho\,\mathbf 1_{\{\left\lVert P\mathcal T^{-1}P\right\rVert\le C(1+L)^C\}} \right] \ge c\,\mathbb E_B\rho. \label{imp:weighted-estimate}$$ Here the expectation uses the boundary law conditional on its four corner boxes. The constants are uniform in all the exterior data just described. Moreover, changing only the single side variables to any other bounded allowed settings, including their artificial absorptions, changes $\rho$ by at most fixed multiplicative factors, pointwise wherever the inverses exist.
- **Acceptance indicator** ([source](#hier:acceptance)): $$A_U=\mathbf 1_{\{U\text{ eligible and allowed}\}} \mathbf 1_{\{t_U\le p_l\mathcal L_U\}}. \label{hier:acceptance}$$ The selectors, acceptance uniforms, and offset are auxiliary randomness; the true potentials are sampled first from their unchanged iid law.
- **Thinning oscillation charge** ([source](#hier:charge-definition)): $$\mathcal C_{n,m}(f) =\sum_{l=n}^{m-1} q_lp_l\, \mathbb E_{\rm off}\sum_{U:\,\operatorname{level}(U)=l} h_{D(U)}^2. \label{hier:charge-definition}$$ The sum includes all tiles of a level, even when the pass acts on only part of the torus; this only enlarges the bound.
- **Local affine jump energy** ([source](#screen:local-energy)): $$d_{n,N}(x)=\frac12 \sum_{a:\,\operatorname{site}(a)=x}\sum_j \|S_{ja}\|^2\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_2(x,\operatorname{site}(j))^2. \label{screen:local-energy}$$ Here $\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_2$ is Euclidean distance formed from the shortest coordinate displacements. Let $d_{n,N}^{\leq D}(x)$ and $d_{n,N}^{>D}(x)$ denote the same sum restricted respectively to jump lengths at most $D$ and greater than $D$.
- **Inverse-square site spectral integral** ([source](#stmt-spec-column)): If $\mu_x$ is the scalar spectral measure of $\delta_x$, then almost surely $$\label{spec:gamma} \Gamma_x(E):=\int_\mathbb R\frac{\mu_x(\,\mathrm de)}{(e-E)^2}<\infty.$$

      "exact_definition": "$$d_{n,N}(x)=\\frac12\n \\sum_{a:\\,\\operatorname{site}(a)=x}\\sum_j\n       |S_{ja}|^2\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_2(x,\\operatorname{site}(j))^2.\n \\label{screen:local-energy}$$ Here $\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_2$ is Euclidean distance formed from the shortest coordinate displacements. Let $d_{n,N}^{\\leq D}(x)$ and $d_{n,N}^{>D}(x)$ denote the same sum restricted respectively to jump lengths at most $D$ and greater than $D$.",
      "interpretation": "At a site, squared port jump distances are weighted by scattering probabilities; averaging these quantities defines the fixed-stage density.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Local affine jump energy",

| q-almost-sure-spectrum | \sigma(H_v) | = | $[-4-h,4+h]$ | The event is almost sure for each fixed disorder strength. |
| q-port-response-contraction | \left\lVert Y^\top GY\right\rVert | ≤ | $1$ | Invertibility and the stated absorption lower bound are required. |
| q-punctured-profile-inverse | \left\lVert(A^0)^{-1}\right\rVert | ≤ | $L^{C_2}$ | The profile parameter is chosen separately at each sufficiently large cell scale. |
| q-branch-interior-inverse | \left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert | ≤ | $2L^{C_2}$ | Design parameters are fixed in the prescribed order before enlarging the cell. |
| q-designed-root-strength | \sigma | = | $\epsilon^2$ | The root strength is the square of the designed boundary-profile scale. |
| q-branch-likelihood-upper-bound | \mathcal L | ≤ | $\exp\bigl(C L^3+C L^2\log L\bigr)$ | Constants are uniform over permitted corner boxes after fixed design choices. |
| q-response-weighted-transmission-lower-bound | \mathbb E\left\lvert t\right\rvert^2 | ≥ | $e^{-C(\log L)^2}\,\mathbb E_B\rho$ | Cell parameters follow the stated order; constants do not use exterior matrix or source norms. |
| q-transmission-relative-closing-error | \mathbb E\bigl[\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert\,\left\lvert t\right\rvert^2\bigr] | ≤ | $e^{-cL}\,\mathbb E\left\lvert t\right\rvert^2$ | The hidden draw has already been integrated in the conditional closing coefficient. |
| q-selector-and-acceptance-probability | q_l=p_l | = | $\exp(-b_l^6)$ | Only sufficiently large fixed starting levels are used. |
| q-accepted-tile-incidence | \mathbb P(A_U=1\mid\text{offset}) | ≤ | $q_l p_l$ | The estimate drops eligibility and validity restrictions to give an upper bound. |
| q-fixed-stage-to-global-density-limit | d | = | $0$ | Each fixed-stage infinite-volume limit precedes the limit over stages. |
| q-true-green-function-fractional-moment | \mathbb E\left\lvert(H_N-E)^{-1}_{xy}\right\rvert^{s/2} | ≤ | $A\exp\{-a\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,y)\}$ | Constants and the volume threshold depend on the fixed disorder, energy and exponent. |
| q-uniform-mask-contraction | F_N(r) | ≤ | $\tfrac12 F_N(r-4R)$ | The stated radius restriction holds after the scale and torus threshold have been fixed. |
| q-rank-one-spectral-average | \int_\mathbb R\mu_a\,\mathrm da | ≤ | $\operatorname{Leb}$ | The inequality compares measures on the real line. |

          "exact_quote": "For fixed $h,E,s$, parameters through $R$ and an integer $N_0$ can be chosen so that, for every $N\\ge N_0$, $$F_N(r)\\le\\tfrac12 F_N(r-4R),\\qquad r\\ge10R.$$ Here $F_N(r)$ is the supremum of $\\mathbb E\\left\\lVert S_{Y'X}\\right\\rVert_{\\rm HS}^s$ over all deterministic artificial masks, all subsets $X,Y'$ lying in radius-$R$ squares, and all centers at distance at least $r$. A supremum over an empty family is zero. Consequently there are constants $K,a_1>0$, independent of $N\\ge N_0$, such that $$\\label{boot:mask-decay}\n                 F_N(r)\\le K R^s e^{-a_1 r/R},\\qquad r\\ge0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-261.2"
        }
      ],

            "exact_quote": "For fixed $h,E,s$,",
            "source_id": "PM-261.2"

    },
    {

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spec-averaging",
          "exact_quote": "Let $H_0$ be bounded self-adjoint and let $\\xi$ be a unit vector. For $a\\in\\mathbb R$, let $\\mu_a$ be the scalar spectral measure of $\\xi$ for $H_0+a\\langle\\xi,\\cdot\\rangle\\xi$. Then, as measures on $\\mathbb R$, $$\\label{spec:average}\n \\int_\\mathbb R\\mu_a\\,\\mathrm da\\le \\operatorname{Leb}.$$ Suppose on a Borel set $B$ the base measure $\\mu_0$ satisfies $\\int(e-E)^{-2}\\mu_0(\\,\\mathrm de)<\\infty$ for Lebesgue-almost every $E\\in B$. For almost every $a$, the restriction of $\\mu_a$ to $B$ is concentrated on eigenvalues of the perturbed operator.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-absorption** [claim](#stmt-mat-ward) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mat-ward): Inverse difference identities and Schur-extension quadratic forms. -> Projected contraction, unitary physical scattering and passive Schur complements.
- **step-port-closure** [claim](#stmt-mat-closure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mat-closure): Woodbury inversion and rational continuation. -> Rational analytic retained-port scattering family.
- **step-single-port-comparison** [claim](#stmt-mat-energy-closure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mat-energy-closure): Expand the energy trace after subtracting the removed datum. -> Conditional energy comparison using the mean closing coefficient.
- **step-cutoff-law** [claim](#stmt-mat-cutoff) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mat-cutoff): Compare the cutoff angular density with Haar measure and estimate the deleted arc. -> Uniform closing error and improved error away from small response.
- **step-weak-resolvent** [claim](#stmt-mat-weak) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mat-weak): Hilbert-space-valued kernel decomposition and positive matrix measures. -> Dimension-independent weak integral and fractional-moment bounds.
- **step-phase-integration** [claim](#stmt-mat-phase) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mat-phase): Choose a determinant logarithm, differentiate its phase and absorb profile derivative errors. -> Integrated inverse trace bounds.
- **step-clipped-geometry** [claim](#stmt-cell-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cell-geometry): Compute corner row spaces and singular values, then inspect side rows. -> Coupled boundary decomposition and inward-neighbor geometry.
- **step-profile-parameter** [claim](#stmt-cell-profile) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cell-profile): Congruence, positive derivative forms and exclusion of short eigenvalue windows. -> A deterministic punctured inverse bound with preserved profile margins.
- **step-branch-law** [claim](#stmt-cell-branch) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cell-branch): Neumann-series stability, residual comparison, root calibration and density multiplication. -> Stable branch draws, compact true potentials and a normalized bounded likelihood.
- **step-absorbed-response-comparison** [claim](#stmt-imp-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-imp-comparison): Inverse difference and projected absorption contraction. -> Multiplicative comparison of projected responses.
- **step-polynomial-comparisons** [claim](#stmt-imp-polynomials) / [proof](#proof-stmt-imp-polynomials): Rescale the box, use scalar norm equivalence and project vector polynomials. -> Norm, derivative and product comparisons.
- **step-slice-resolution** [claim](#stmt-imp-resolution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-imp-resolution): Use a short interval around a maximizing point and polynomial averaging. -> A supremum-to-integral bound at the denominator resolution.
- **step-corner-schur-control** [claim](#stmt-imp-schur-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-imp-schur-estimates): Apply absorption to a Schur extension and differentiate supported mixed columns. -> Coupling derivatives, absorption floor and scalar shift integral.
- **step-weighted-impedance** [claim](#stmt-imp-weighted) / [proof](#pm-h-22): Separate appreciable and small coupling, control weighted exceptional slices, then restore the common side shift. -> A projected inverse bound on a set retaining a fixed fraction of response weight.
- **step-boundary-schur-interface** [claim](#stmt-imp-schur-interface) / [proof](#proof-stmt-imp-schur-interface): Generic invertibility of missing directions and block source elimination. -> Passive effective boundary matrix and normalized source.
- **step-cell-transmission** [claim](#stmt-trans-cell) / [proof](#pm-h-30): Find regular boundary and shift data, propagate a source along a resolvent path using independent jitters, then restore absorption and the calibrated root. -> Transmission lower bound and exponentially small relative closing error.
- **step-interior-envelope** [claim](#stmt-trans-envelope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trans-envelope): Replace active ports by unit absorption with true references, integrate inverse traces and sum over all internal subsets. -> A common pointwise envelope with controlled true-law expectation.
- **step-conditional-sampling** [claim](#stmt-hier-sampling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hier-sampling): Use corner coincidence and likelihood normalization under conditional product laws. -> Independent eligible-tile sampling and exact conditional branch acceptance.
- **step-hidden-root** [claim](#stmt-hier-hidden) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hier-hidden): Identify the remaining root likelihood factor under permitted conditioning. -> Preserved cutoff Cauchy root law and hidden-draw independence.
- **step-parent-cap** [claim](#stmt-hier-cap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hier-cap): Compare child-survival envelopes with an accepted active parent in the same ensemble. -> Retention-proportional exterior-mass cap.
- **step-thinning-comparison** [claim](#stmt-hier-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hier-cap-2): Differentiate the finite thinning polynomial, apply conditional closure and integrate the capped interpolation cost. -> Energy comparison for every permitted pass prefix.
- **step-charge-tails** [claim](#stmt-hier-charges) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hier-charges): Count tiles meeting global or localized Lipschitz data and sum the incidence tails. -> Vanishing normalized and localized charge tails.
- **step-generic-observation** [claim](#stmt-screen-observation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-screen-observation): Support-pattern counting and null images of lower-dimensional potential maps, followed by a finite-dimensional operator bound. -> Almost-sure core observation and deterministic high-quantile constants.
- **step-projected-screening** [claim](#stmt-screen-projected) / [proof](#proof-stmt-screen-projected): Combine observation with absorption, then conjugate by a good-edge distance and use a shield cutoff. -> Projected inverse bounds, attenuation and shared-shield comparison.
- **step-full-path-cost** [claim](#stmt-screen-paths) / [proof](#proof-stmt-screen-paths): Force separated smaller crossing failures, truncate hazards and iterate the independent-pair recursion. -> Polynomial crossing costs with polynomially small exceptional probability.
- **step-wall-cost** [claim](#stmt-screen-walls) / [proof](#proof-stmt-screen-walls): Find a buffered annulus inside a retained strip, then repeat the crossing recursion. -> Wall crossing estimates uniform as the wall scale grows.
- **step-fixed-stage-limits** [claim](#stmt-screen-limits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-screen-limits): Bound jump tails, approximate by finite-dependence local functions, remove probes and average periodic conditional means. -> Finite fixed-stage density and uniform spatial convergence.
- **step-profile-bound** [claim](#stmt-screen-profiles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-screen-profiles): Separate long jumps from short jumps and average local gradient weights using fixed-stage approximants. -> Macroscopic profile energy upper bounds.
- **step-affine-limit** [claim](#stmt-screen-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-screen-limit): Apply thinning to periodic chord coordinates, derive almost monotonicity and compare upper and lower limits. -> A finite nonnegative limiting affine density.
- **step-adaptive-adjustment** [claim](#stmt-van-adjustment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-van-adjustment): Choose the sign of a supported affine adjustment and compare complementary thinning passes. -> Arbitrarily small expected local affine first variation.
- **step-probe-barrier** [claim](#stmt-van-barrier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-van-barrier): Count simple cheap paths and convert a cutoff commutator into an absorbed probe source. -> Exponential barrier failure bound and exterior-to-interior response comparison.
- **step-good-event** [claim](#stmt-van-good) / [proof](#proof-stmt-van-good): Combine logarithmic capacity, retained interior screening and weighted far-wall transmission bounds. -> A good event with small leakage and positive bounded internal jump energy.
- **step-analytic-trace** [claim](#stmt-van-analytic-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-van-analytic-bounds): Use determinant and adjugate to obtain analyticity, compare an almost-unitary central block and bound boundary moments by weak resolvent estimates. -> Nonzero analytic central value and bounded fractional boundary moments.
- **step-vanishing-density** [claim](#stmt-van-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-van-analytic-bounds-2): Transfer first-variation smallness through support-preserving potential twists, integrate bounded twists and Cauchy tails, then contradict submean. -> The limiting affine density vanishes.
- **step-mixture-domination** [claim](#stmt-boot-twist) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boot-twist): Close newly filled probes with a common phase and obtain pointwise lower domination of the shifted mixture. -> Old-mask transmission moment bound.
- **step-tilted-site-control** [claim](#stmt-boot-tilted) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boot-tilted): One-site Schur representation, plateau numerator control, conditional moment comparison and iteration over distinct bad sites. -> Uniform tilted barrier probabilities and reciprocal-profile moments.
- **step-small-return** [claim](#stmt-boot-return) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boot-return): Compare the auxiliary hierarchy to the true filled system using an inner barrier cutoff and rare large-selector events. -> Uniformly arbitrarily small mean-square annular return.
- **step-mask-contraction** [claim](#stmt-boot-contraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boot-contraction): Combine direct attenuation with independent inside/outside resampling, control tilted exceptions and cover the annulus by source sectors. -> Uniform mask contraction and iterated exponential transmission decay.
- **step-true-fractional-decay** [claim](#stmt-boot-fractional) / [proof](#pm-h-55): Remove endpoint probes using an exact compression identity and uniform real two-site moment integration. -> Fixed-real-energy exponential fractional moments for the undamped torus Green function.
- **step-infinite-column** [claim](#stmt-spec-column) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-column): Couple centered tori to the infinite field, use weighted fractional sums, choose a bounded subsequence and pass each local equation to the limit. -> Square-summable inverse column and finite inverse-square spectral integral.
- **step-spectral-averaging** [claim](#stmt-spec-averaging) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-averaging): Rank-one resolvent inversion, Poisson integration and construction of eigenvectors at zeros of the perturbation denominator. -> Lebesgue domination and almost-everywhere pure-point perturbed measures on the specified set.
- **step-pure-point-readout** [claim](#stmt-spec-readout) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-readout): Establish true-operator measurability, apply Fubini and rank-one averaging, remove endpoints and intersect over sites. -> Pure-point site measures and a complete eigenbasis.
- **step-spectrum-interval** [claim](#stmt-spec-spectrum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-spectrum): Use independent nearly constant boxes and cutoff plane waves for approximate eigenvectors; then identify the spectral closure. -> The full deterministic spectral interval and dense eigenvalues.
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-main-theorem) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-spectrum): Combine spectral purity with the spectrum determination. -> The stated planar pure-point theorem at fixed positive disorder.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-physical-scattering**: For a real symmetric physical site matrix, real port columns, and an existing absorbed inverse, the physical scattering matrix is symmetric and unitary.
- **inv-common-phase-closure**: Closing the designated ports and applying the prescribed real diagonal shifts gives the same retained scattering matrix as the common-phase closure formula, wherever the required inverses exist.
- **inv-dirichlet-trace**: For real scalar port data, the scattering Dirichlet energy equals the trace expression obtained from unitarity.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-test-spectral-certificate**: Treat agreement of finite port calculations as a certificate of the infinite-volume pure-point theorem. -> Exact finite algebra checks neither the probabilistic hierarchy nor the infinite-volume spectral passage.
- **anti-singular-closure**: Apply inverse formulas at a singular physical matrix or singular closure denominator. -> The inverse defining the response does not exist; substituting a generalized inverse changes the stated construction.
- **anti-complex-port-transpose**: Use complex port columns while retaining the physical transpose formulas and their unitarity guarantee. -> The physical argument uses real port columns, so this substitution does not preserve its hypotheses.
- **anti-dynamical-readout**: Infer time-uniform transport bounds from the local scattering checks or the main pure-point conclusion. -> Neither the finite algebra nor the fixed-energy theorem provides the asserted time-evolution control.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite common-phase closure lemma supplies an explicit rational construction. The executable scope is its local matrix identity, together with physical scattering symmetry, unitarity, and the Dirichlet trace identity. It does not certify the infinite-volume spectral theorem. The source states: "The right-hand side is analytic for $\left\lvert z\right\rvert<1$."

```python
# INPUTS: JSON containing H, E, ports, close, w, and real port data f.
# PRECONDITIONS: H is symmetric rational; couplings are positive rational; both physical inverses exist.
# OUTPUTS: Retained scattering matrix as rational real/imaginary pairs, its Dirichlet energy, and closed_port_count.
# TIME: Finite exact matrix algebra; no complexity bound is asserted.
# SPACE: Finite exact matrices; no storage bound is asserted.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational complex arithmetic.
# FAILURE: Invalid inputs or singular required inverses cause a nonzero exit.
import json
import sys
import sympy as sp

Q = sp.Rational
I = sp.I

def rational(x):
    if isinstance(x, bool) or not isinstance(x, (str, int)):
        raise ValueError('rational input required')
    return Q(x)

def run(d):
    raw = d['H']
    n = len(raw)
    if n == 0 or any(len(row) != n for row in raw):
        raise ValueError('square nonempty H required')
    H = sp.Matrix([[rational(x) for x in row] for row in raw])
    if H != H.T:
        raise ValueError('symmetric H required')
    E = rational(d['E'])
    w = rational(d['w'])
    ports = d['ports']
    p = len(ports)
    Y = sp.zeros(n, p)
    for a, port in enumerate(ports):
        site = port['site']
        c = rational(port['coupling'])
        if type(site) is not int or not 0 <= site < n or c <= 0:
            raise ValueError('invalid real port')
        Y[site, a] = c
    closed = d['close']
    if any(type(a) is not int or not 0 <= a < p for a in closed):
        raise ValueError('invalid closed index')
    if len(set(closed)) != len(closed):
        raise ValueError('duplicate closed index')
    f = [rational(x) for x in d['f']]
    if len(f) != p:
        raise ValueError('invalid data length')
    L = H - E * sp.eye(n) - I * Y * Y.T
    G = L.inv()
    S = sp.eye(p) + 2 * I * Y.T * G * Y
    retained = [a for a in range(p) if a not in closed]
    Yclosed = Y[:, closed]
    Lfinal = L + (w + I) * Yclosed * Yclosed.T
    if Lfinal.det() == 0:
        raise ValueError('singular final physical matrix')
    A = S.extract(retained, retained)
    if closed:
        T = S.extract(retained, closed)
        B = S.extract(closed, closed)
        z = (w + I) / (w - I)
        middle = (sp.eye(len(closed)) - z * B).inv()
        retained_S = A + T * z * middle * T.T
    else:
        retained_S = A
    retained_S = retained_S.applyfunc(sp.simplify)
    if retained:
        F = sp.diag(*[f[a] for a in retained])
        energy = sp.simplify(sp.trace(F * F - F * retained_S * F * retained_S.conjugate().T))
    else:
        energy = Q(0)
    if sp.im(energy) != 0:
        raise ValueError('nonreal energy')
    entries = []
    for a in range(len(retained)):
        row = []
        for b in range(len(retained)):
            value = sp.expand_complex(retained_S[a, b])
            row.append([str(sp.simplify(sp.re(value))), str(sp.simplify(sp.im(value)))])
        entries.append(row)
    return {'S': entries, 'energy': str(energy), 'closed_port_count': len(closed)}

print(json.dumps(run(json.load(sys.stdin)), sort_keys=True))
```

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-uniform-independent-diagonal-disorder** (hypothesis): The spectral localization conclusion concerns independent uniform site potentials and deterministic nearest-neighbor hopping on the square lattice. Extension to other single-site laws, correlations or random hopping is not established here. Blocked use: ['Certifying localization for Gaussian or Bernoulli disorder using this theorem.', 'Applying the spectral conclusion to correlated potentials or random hopping.']
- **limit-fixed-positive-disorder-event** (formal_scope): The almost-sure spectral assertion fixes a positive disorder strength, and its probability-one event may depend on that strength. Blocked use: ['Claiming a common probability-one event simultaneously for all positive disorder strengths.', 'Including zero disorder in the localization assertion.']
- **limit-spectral-type-not-dynamics** (formal_scope): Pure-point spectral type and dense eigenvalues do not supply the time-evolution or dynamical-localization guarantees excluded by the source. Blocked use: ['Guaranteeing time-uniform wavepacket confinement.', 'Interpreting vanishing auxiliary affine energy as an established physical diffusion theorem.']
- **limit-spectrum-versus-eigenvalues** (edge_case): Completeness of the eigenvectors does not assert that every point of the spectral interval is an eigenvalue. Blocked use: ['Enumerating every spectral point as an eigenvalue.', 'Treating density of eigenvalues as equality between the spectrum and the eigenvalue set.']
- **limit-fixed-energy-fractional-moments** (hypothesis): The Green-function decay theorem fixes an interior real energy and the stated fractional exponent. Its constants and torus threshold depend on the fixed parameters. Blocked use: ['Using an energy-independent decay constant or torus threshold.', 'Applying the interior-energy proof directly at spectral endpoints.', 'Replacing the stated fractional moment by a stronger moment without a separate estimate.']
- **limit-invertibility-and-passive-coupling** (hypothesis): The finite response and Schur formulas require the relevant inverses. The absorption estimates require passivity and the specified absorption lower bound; physical symmetry and unitarity additionally use the real physical coupling convention. Blocked use: ['Substituting a generalized inverse at a singular matrix while claiming the same identity.', 'Applying passive estimates to an active exterior.', 'Claiming physical unitarity with arbitrary additional absorption or complex port columns.']
- **limit-common-phase-closure** (hypothesis): The block closure construction uses a common normalized real closing parameter and scales each diagonal shift by its closed port strength. Analyticity inside the disk does not remove exceptional real closing values. Blocked use: ['Assigning unrelated phases to closed ports while using the common-phase formula.', 'Assuming every real closing value yields an invertible physical matrix.', 'Merging distinct channels that share a physical site.']
- **limit-cutoff-law-and-small-strength** (hypothesis): The closing-error estimates use the specified smooth compact cutoff and sufficiently small port strength. The improved error estimate additionally requires the stated local-response threshold. Blocked use: ['Replacing the conditional closing law by an arbitrary bounded distribution.', 'Using the improved closing error throughout the small-response region.', 'Applying the asymptotic cutoff estimate at an unverified port strength.']
- **limit-cell-design-order-and-boundary-conditioning** (hypothesis): The resonant branch and transmission estimates require the prescribed cell geometry, permitted corner boxes, ordered design choices and small artificial absorption. The comparison of the branch with the interior envelope requires matching corner-box conditionings. Blocked use: ['Choosing shell depth from an exponent that itself depends on that depth.', 'Comparing branch and envelope weights under different corner-box conditionings.', 'Using the local transmission estimate with unrestricted artificial absorption.']
- **limit-hidden-root-information** (hypothesis): The conditional hidden-root law excludes the numerical acceptance uniform. Adaptive thinning data must preserve that law and remain independent of the thinning marks used by the pass. Blocked use: ['Exposing the numerical acceptance uniform before using the cutoff Cauchy root law.', 'Choosing adaptive data by inspecting still-hidden root potentials.', 'Recomputing branch decisions during the thinning interpolation.']
- **limit-projected-screening-and-intact-tests** (hypothesis): Screening controls the projected response on counted good cores and ports. A partially thinned or restricted system must preserve the complete observation neighborhood and the tested cell-port data. Blocked use: ['Inferring a norm bound for the full inverse from projected screening.', 'Counting a core after deleting a required neighboring equation or changing its tested references.', 'Using a shield comparison when the counted tests are not shared and usable.']
- **limit-generic-observation-not-universal** (nonconstructive): Local observation holds almost surely under the true potential density. A deterministic observation constant is selected through a probability quantile rather than a bound valid for every deterministic potential configuration. Blocked use: ['Treating the observation estimate as deterministic unique continuation for arbitrary potentials.', 'Using an unspecified observation quantile as a certified numerical tolerance.']
- **limit-fixed-stage-then-spatial-limits** (approximation): The starting stage is fixed before observation constants, local approximations and spatial limits are chosen. Convergence over stages is taken afterwards; the source supplies no rate uniform in the starting stage. Blocked use: ['Interchanging the stage and infinite-volume limits without additional justification.', 'Turning asymptotic profile bounds into finite-size stopping criteria.', 'Claiming that the compact-profile upper bound is an equality or a continuum convergence theorem.']
- **limit-planar-capacity-mechanism** (formal_scope): The vanishing argument uses the small capacity cost of a logarithmic voltage profile in the planar geometry. It does not establish the same localization conclusion on higher-dimensional lattices. Blocked use: ['Transferring the vanishing-density proof unchanged to higher dimensions.', 'Treating the auxiliary affine density as a dimension-independent localization criterion.']
- **limit-tilted-law-and-resampling** (hypothesis): The transmission tilt requires a positive normalizing expectation. Independence is not asserted under the tilted law or conditional on the original barrier event; the resampling factorization is unconditional. Blocked use: ['Normalizing a tilt when the transmission expectation vanishes.', 'Factoring transmission moments conditional on the barrier event.', 'Treating tilted site potentials as independent.']
- **limit-endpoint-removal-and-spectral-readout** (formal_scope): Removal of endpoint probes uses separate real-site moment estimates. The infinite-volume spectral conclusion then requires a square-summable column, true-operator measurability, Fubini and rank-one averaging; finite scattering checks alone do not establish it. Blocked use: ['Promoting a finite closure test to certification of the complete spectral theorem.', 'Skipping spectral averaging after a fixed-energy resolvent estimate.', 'Assuming a single site vector is cyclic for the full planar operator.']
- **limit-nonconstructive-global-constants** (nonconstructive): The global proof selects scales through qualitative limits and a contradiction, and extracts the infinite-volume column along a configuration-dependent subsequence. It does not provide a finite eigenbasis-enumeration procedure or an explicit numerical schedule for the global decay constants. Blocked use: ['Using the existence of sufficiently large scales as an executable stopping rule.', 'Claiming that finite exact algebra determines the infinite-volume eigenbasis.', 'Assigning numerical global localization constants without additional estimates.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 261 (`lean/docs/261.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Pure-Point Spectrum for the Two-Dimensional Anderson Model at Every Positive Disorder](../../preprints/Pure-Point-Spectrum-for-the-Two-Dimensional-Anderson-Model-at-Every-Positive-Disorder-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The linked formalization proves a supporting spectral statement for the nearest-neighbor Anderson operator on $\mathbb Z^2$. For every disorder strength $h>0$ with independent site potentials uniform on $[-h,h]$, it constructs the bounded self-adjoint operator almost surely and identifies its spectrum as the real interval $[-4-h,4+h]$.
>
> This selected statement identifies the spectral set. It does not assert the pure-point spectral type claimed in the accompanying paper.

Comparator source `PlanarAndersonSpectrum.lean` (958 bytes, sha256 `476c0769acc7d2ba1dbb2ae1b451a4c03786cc79df4affaa9cdd03c5669ff2cf`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section
open MeasureTheory
open scoped ENNReal

namespace PlanarAnderson

abbrev Site := ℤ × ℤ
abbrev Configuration := Site → ℝ
abbrev Hilbert := lp (fun _ : Site => ℂ) 2

def disorderLaw (h : ℝ) : Measure Configuration :=
  Measure.infinitePi (fun _ : Site => ProbabilityTheory.cond volume (Set.Icc (-h) h))

def IsAndersonOperator (v : Configuration) (H : Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert) : Prop :=
  ∀ (u : Hilbert) (x : Site),
    (H u) x = u (x.1 + 1, x.2) + u (x.1 - 1, x.2) +
      u (x.1, x.2 + 1) + u (x.1, x.2 - 1) + (v x : ℂ) * u x

def spectralInterval (h : ℝ) : Set ℂ :=
  {z | z.im = 0 ∧ -4 - h ≤ z.re ∧ z.re ≤ 4 + h}

theorem anderson_spectrum_ae {h : ℝ} (hh : 0 < h) :
    ∀ᵐ v ∂disorderLaw h, ∃ H : Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert,
      IsAndersonOperator v H ∧ IsSelfAdjoint H ∧ spectrum ℂ H = spectralInterval h := by
  sorry

end PlanarAnderson

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For each fixed positive disorder strength, we prove that the nearest-neighbor Anderson operator on the square lattice with independent uniform site potentials almost surely has pure-point spectral type throughout its spectrum. This resolves the pure-point assertion of the two-dimensional Anderson localization conjecture for the uniform single-site law.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
